# Design decisions

One row per decision that creates a rule. A decision that is a picture belongs on a
canvas and then in a project's own documentation; this file is for the rules.

Newest last. Never rewrite a row: supersede it with a new one that says so.

| Date | Question | Answer | Rule it creates |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-08 | One repository per Orbiters project, or one for all of them? | One monorepo, with a directory per project under `projects/`. | A project owns everything under `projects/<name>/`, including its own docs, Dockerfiles and deploy scripts. |
| 2026-09-08 | Per-project lockfiles, or one per language for the whole repository? | One `uv.lock` and one `pnpm-lock.yaml`, both at the root. | Two projects can never resolve the same library at two versions. A project needing an incompatible pin leaves the workspace, with the reason written down. |
| 2026-09-08 | Where do build-and-test dependency versions live? | In `pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s `catalog:`. | A package writes `"typescript": "catalog:"`. What a project *ships* stays in its own `package.json`; what it needs in order to be built, linted and tested is monorepo policy. |
| 2026-09-08 | Do a project's design documents move to a shared `docs/` at the root? | No. They stay inside the project. | `docs/` at the root is about the monorepo only. A test that reads a note as a contract keeps working, and a project stays extractable. |
| 2026-09-08 | Per-project `pyproject.toml` for pytest and mypy configuration? | No. Both stay at the root and name each project's paths in full. | Everything runs from the repository root; CI narrows to one project by passing its paths. A config that only resolves when you have `cd`'d into a directory silently checks nothing when you have not. |
| 2026-09-08 | One CI workflow per project, or one workflow calling shared gates? | One `ci.yml`, calling `_python-gate.yml` and `_node-gate.yml` through `workflow_call`. | Adding a project costs a filter and a job, never another CI workflow. Deploy is the exception and is always per project. |
| 2026-09-08 | Which status check does the trunk require? | `ci`, the aggregate job, and only it. | Job names below `ci` can change forever without a ruleset edit. (Not yet enforceable: the org is on the Free plan and the repository is private, so rulesets are unavailable.) |
| 2026-09-08 | Rename the `PIGROCRM_*` namespace now that the repository is not just PigroCRM? | No. | Environment variables, distribution names, the CLI and the databases stay project-scoped. The project is still called PigroCRM. |
| 2026-09-08 | Which language does repository-facing text use? | English, everywhere: docs, README, AGENTS.md, issues, PR and commit messages, code comments. | A product's user-facing strings stay in the language its users read, which for PigroCRM is Italian. Existing Italian specs under `projects/pigrocrm/docs/superpowers/` are not retranslated; anything written from now on is English. |
| 2026-09-08 | One Linear project for the monorepo, or one per project? | One per project, in the `Orbiters` team. | A new project opens its own Linear project (`docs/adding-a-project.md` §9). `Area` labels are shared across projects; priority is Linear's native field, not a label. |
| 2026-09-09 | When does a project deploy? | Preview on every push to `main` that touched it; production only on a project-scoped version tag `<project>-v<semver>`. | A push to main means the trunk moved, which is worth seeing on a real server and is not worth putting in front of the person who uses the product every day. A bare `v1.2.0` in a monorepo does not say which project it releases. |
| 2026-09-09 | Where does per-environment deploy configuration live? | In GitHub Environments (`<project>-preview`, `<project>-production`), each holding the same four secrets: `DEPLOY_HOST`, `DEPLOY_USER`, `DEPLOY_PATH`, `DEPLOY_SSH_KEY`. | Prefixed repository secrets would grow one name per project per environment. Four names that never change scale; forty names do not. Environments also carry deployment history and are where a production reviewer goes once the plan allows it. Callers must pass `secrets: inherit` or a reusable workflow reads them as empty strings. |
| 2026-09-09 | One deploy workflow, or one per project? | One shared mechanism (`_deploy-compose.yml`) plus a short caller per project. Deploy is still never triggered across projects. | The earlier rule said the whole workflow is per project, which duplicated ssh, rsync, the CI gate and the health check on every new project. The trigger stays per project, which is the part that must never be shared; the steps do not. |
| 2026-09-09 | The public pages lived inside the CRM's web app. Where do they belong? | Their own project, `projects/website`: it is joinorbiters.com, a site of its own that will grow past a landing page, not a corner of the CRM. | It builds in 300ms with no framework, and it was being carried by an application build that takes twenty times that. Extracting it also removed 394 bytes of dead CSS from the application, generated by Tailwind scanning the website's own JavaScript (`addEventListener('resize')` produced a `.resize` utility nothing rendered). One deployable still serves both today: ORB-12 carries splitting the serving, which moves two live domains. |
| 2026-09-09 | Two surfaces, one brand: where do the palette and the typeface live? | `shared/brand`, a workspace package both projects depend on. The application reads it as part of its Tailwind theme; the website extracts it at build time, having no Tailwind at all. | There must be one source of colour and no copy. `--radius` deliberately stayed with the application: the website's system is square by design. Verified by diffing the emitted CSS before and after the move: byte-identical, same font asset hash. |
| 2026-09-09 | What language is this repository written in? | English, everywhere: docs, code, comments, commit messages, Linear. The only exception is what the product says to its users, which stays in the user's language (Italian for PigroCRM). | Supersedes the 2026-09-08 row, which only covered documentation. A repository half in one language cannot be handed to a contributor, and this one is meant to be open-sourceable. The dated records under `docs/superpowers/` are grandfathered: rewriting a record is how it stops being a record. |
| 2026-09-09 | May a project be a single package at its own root, instead of `apps/*` plus `packages/*`? | Yes, when it is one artifact. `projects/website` is four HTML pages and three scripts. | An `apps/` directory with exactly one entry in it is ceremony. The pnpm workspace matches `projects/*` as well, and a directory without a package.json is simply not a package. |
| 2026-09-09 | Where does the ChatGPT Ads pixel go, and who holds its key? | The pixel initialises on the two pages an ad can land on (`index.html`, `orbiters.html`) and on neither legal page; the conversion is measured twice -- in the browser and from the API -- sharing one event id; the Conversions API key lives only in the server's `.env`, and `pigrocrm conversions-check` is how it is verified. | A tracker on a privacy policy cannot be defended, and the browser event is the one that gets blocked, so the server half is what makes the numbers real. One shared id is what keeps the pair from counting as two. The key has full read and write access to the conversion source and cannot be scoped, so nothing that is served to a browser or committed may contain it. |
| 2026-09-09 | Does the measurement pixel wait for consent? | Yes, and it is not loaded at all until somebody says yes: `consent.js` shows a small notice and injects the SDK only on acceptance, so before a decision the browser makes no request to OpenAI. A refusal is remembered and not asked again. | A tracker that fetches its own script before the answer has already handed a third party the visitor's IP and user agent, which is the thing consent exists to decide -- so `oaiq('consent', false)` after the snippet would have been a notice about something that already happened. Refusing has to cost the same one click as accepting, or it is not consent. |
| 2026-09-09 | Where does the root installation log in, and where do its cookies live? | At the bare `/app/login` (and `/app/registrati`), which nginx never redirects under the root's name; the root's session cookies are `Path=/` even when the request wore `/<root_slug>/`. | After a logout nobody is in a space, so the address must not name one. One jar at `/` is the only thing the bare login and the aliased app can both read; a space keeps `/<slug>/`, and the API reads the most specific cookie first so a root cookie never shadows a space's own. |
| 2026-09-09 | Does PigroCRM have a running timer, after slice 4 §13 argued against one? | Yes, one per user, as a server row (`time_timers`) that becomes an ordinary time entry when stopped; the weekly grid stays as the second view of `/app/ore`. | Ivan asked for the Toggl/Clockify shape. A row on the server is one mechanism, not three: the closed browser and the second device both just read it back, and `stop` goes through `TimeEntryService.create`, so nothing about how an hour is valued changed. Spec: `projects/pigrocrm/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-09-time-tracking-timer-and-register-design.md`. |
| 2026-09-09 | The website was built into the CRM's image and served at its document root. Split the serving too? | Yes: its own image, its own compose stack on 127.0.0.1:8082, its own vhost and its own `website-v<semver>` tag. | A copy change was waiting for a CRM release, and a CRM rollback took the public site with it. The two have different release rhythms and nothing in common but a brand. `_deploy-compose.yml` already took a project and an environment, so this cost a caller and two GitHub environments, not new machinery. |
| 2026-09-09 | After the split, `pigro.joinorbiters.com/pigrocrm`, `/privacy`, `/termini` and `/orbiters` still answered from the CRM's image. Keep a second copy? | No: 301 to joinorbiters.com, and the CRM's image stops building the site. | A policy page with two live copies is a policy page that gets edited in one of them. The redirect keeps every old link alive at the cost of one hop. |
| 2026-09-09 | One deployable starts serving what another served. Which lands first? | The new one goes up and takes the name first; the old one stops building it afterwards, in a later commit. | Production here is deployed from `main` by hand, so a merge is a release. Doing it in the other order took joinorbiters.com down to redirects for twenty minutes (ORB-16). The safe direction costs nothing: an unpointed container can run for days. |
| 2026-09-09 | Where does Orbiters live in the monorepo? | In `projects/hub`, as a product of its own: its own Postgres, its own API (`orbiters_api`), its own MCP server (`orbiters_mcp`), its own Alembic history. Nothing of it stays in `pigrocrm-*`, and neither product imports the other (`projects/hub/ruff.toml` bans the `pigrocrm*` roots). | Ivan asked for the split on 2026-09-09: the community and the CRM are two products with two audiences, and a change to one must never be a deploy of the other. The signup table is adopted where it stands (migration 0001 is all `IF NOT EXISTS`); `pigrocrm conversions-check` is now `orbiters conversions-check`. Spec: `projects/hub/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-09-orbiters-hub-design.md`. |
| 2026-09-09 | What is a «logged work day», and what is a «deadline»? | A day is hours on a deal -- an ordinary `time_entries` row, with «giornata» as an eight-hour preset -- and a deadline is an `attivita` (slice 7's table, built now) with a nullable `scadenza`. The calendar also *shows* invoice due dates and cannot change them. | The hours model requires a deal because the P&L and the invoicing do (slice 4), and a second «day» concept outside it would be a diary that no report can read. A due date on an invoice is a property of the document, decided at issue: editable from a view showing neither number nor amount, it would be a fiscal document changed from the wrong place. |
| 2026-09-09 | Does the trunk tier run everything on every commit, or is it path-scoped like the PR tier? | Path-scoped, by the same filters, with `all=true` when the push has no reachable base commit. | This repository is private, so every Actions minute is billed, and it burned 1,564 of a 2,000/month allowance in eight days. Running the CRM's twelve-minute suite on a push that only touched the website bought nothing. The property given up is "the trunk proves the whole tree on every commit"; what keeps it honest is that a change to a shared root file (`uv.lock`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `pyproject.toml`, `shared/brand/`) selects everything. |
| 2026-09-09 | A deploy refuses a commit CI has not passed. Does it wait for CI, or is it triggered by it? | Triggered by it: the preview runs on `workflow_run` when CI concludes green on `main`, reads what changed from the artifact `changes` published, and deploys `github.event.workflow_run.head_sha`. Production stays on the tag push. | Waiting was a `sleep 30` loop on a metered runner: measured on one preview, 458 of 514 billed seconds were the wait and 51 were the deploy. The guarantee is unchanged, since `_deploy-compose.yml` still asks the API which run covers that commit. Two consequences are load-bearing: `github.sha` on a `workflow_run` event is the branch tip at event time, so the verified sha has to be passed explicitly, and such a workflow only ever runs in its default-branch version, so these files cannot be tested from a branch. |
| 2026-09-09 | May a release ever be done by hand on the server? | No. Every stack, preview or production, is released only by its deploy workflow: preview on a green trunk run, production on a `<project>-v<semver>` tag. A deploy that is not armed gets armed (environment, the four secrets, the variable); nothing ships by hand in the meantime. Since 2026-09-09 all three stacks (`pigrocrm`, `website`, `hub`) are armed and have released this way. | Ivan, 2026-09-09, twice: «rilasciare fatto tramite CI sempre», then «va rilasciato tutto, no deploy manuali». `docker compose up`, `rsync` or `git pull` on the server are not a release; ssh is for reading state and for operations that are not a release (cron, `.env`, `createadmin`, the vhost). The deploy refuses a commit whose CI is not green, and that guarantee only exists while the deploy is the one path. |
| 2026-09-09 | May the cookie notice cover the page while it waits for an answer? | No. On a phone the whole community page fits one screen, so what the fixed notice covers stays covered. While it is up, consent.js measures it and writes `--consent-room` on the root; system.css spends it as a spacer at the end of the body, not as body padding, so neither page's own padding arithmetic has to know about it. Both go away with the notice. | The notice never covers content, on any page that carries it. `body::after` in system.css is the one place the room is spent, and no other rule owns that pseudo-element. |
| 2026-09-09 | Who is Orbiters for, and where is that written down? | `docs/design/positioning.md`: software developers, AI engineers, CTOs and fractional tech roles working on their own account, at a seniority where a company can hand them a project. «Freelance» stays the legal and fiscal category and stops being the headline. | Every public surface follows the statement: titles, meta and Open Graph, the two `h1`s, the calls to action, the hub's public pages, MCP descriptions. A copy change that departs from it changes the statement first. Routes, API and database vocabulary keep `freelance` as the category name (ORB-24 point 4); `shared/brand` is unchanged by this decision, and the identity verdict in the same file is an argument, not a change. |
| 2026-09-09 | Under the forfettario, does every line carry `N2.2`, or does the customer's country decide? | The country decides: `N2.2` with the profile's L. 190/2014 declaration for an Italian customer (an empty country is Italy, the column's default); `N2.1` for a customer established abroad, with the annotation art. 21 c. 6-bis DPR 633/1972 prescribes: "inversione contabile" for a customer in another EU member state, "operazione non soggetta" outside the EU. `issue` asks the regime again for every stored line and refuses, before the number is consumed, when a line disagrees; `replace_lines` is the remedy. The PDF footer prints the declaration the lines carry, not the profile's text. | A service to a business outside Italy is outside the territorial scope of Italian VAT (art. 7-ter), and the accountant's register already says N2.1 for those invoices (ORB-32). The regime strategy takes the customer's `nazione`, keyword-only and without a default, so nothing can get the domestic answer by omission; `InvoiceLineIn` still refuses a caller-supplied `natura`. The texts and the EU set are constants, not profile columns, because the profile describes the issuer and this depends on the customer. Spec 7.2 of slice 3 recorded the old rule and is not rewritten. |
| 2026-09-09 | For a customer outside Italy, who applies the SdI's `00000` convention for `CAP`: the person entering the record, or the exporter? | The exporter. `_sede` writes `CAP` as `00000` and omits `Provincia` whenever `Nazione` is not `IT`, whatever the customer row holds, and appends the real postcode to `Indirizzo` (`"1 Old Street, EC1V 9HL"`) so the document keeps it; a record that already holds `00000` gets the address alone. `check_party_exportable` measures the same composite against the 60-character bound and requires `comune` of every party, so a foreign address it accepts is one `export_xml` can serialise. An Italian CAP that is not five digits stays refused before the number is consumed. | The customer record holds the address as it is, the PDF prints it, and the file says what the technical specifications for FatturaPA 1.2.x prescribe for a foreign address (`CAP` `00000`, no `Provincia`, `IdPaese` the customer's country). Asking a London company to type `00000` as its postcode was a lie in the record (ORB-38); refusing a real postcode after `issue` had spent the number left annulment as the only remedy. Rule: what the pre-check accepts, the writer serialises, and the one place the two texts differ (`Indirizzo` with the postcode) is a single shared function. |
| 2026-09-09 | Two of us run agents against one board, and each took cards the other had filed for himself (ORB-25, and the same in the other direction). What decides who may work an issue? | The `assignee` field, as a claim: an agent works a card assigned to the account its session writes as, or one with no assignee that it filed itself, or one labelled `parallel` that nobody has claimed. Every issue is filed with an assignee, and the first read of a session establishes which account that is (`get_user` with `"me"`, keeping the id). | A card assigned to somebody else is untouched: no assignee change, no status change, no branch, no PR, nothing re-parented under it, a comment at most, and being asked for it by name does not make it yours. The project lead does not own the project's issues; the lead decides, the assignee works. `parallel` opens an unclaimed card to whoever is free, whoever filed it, and never reopens a claimed one. |
| 2026-09-09 | A production deploy refuses a commit whose `ci.yml` run is not green. Which run, now that the trunk tier is path-scoped? | The run of the ref being deployed. `ci.yml` runs on every `<project>-v*` tag with every job (`all=true`), and `_deploy-compose.yml` selects the run whose head branch is the ref it deploys: the tag for production, `main` for a preview, which is the run that triggered it. | A docs-only commit after a red change to a project gets a trunk run where every job of that project is skipped and `ci` concludes success; a tag on that commit shipped the red code (ORB-28). A release now costs one full CI run, all projects, and the deploy runner waits for it; releases are rare, and the alternative, asking the API which jobs ran, puts a per-project job list into the shared mechanism. |
| 2026-09-09 | Invoicing runs late: August's work is issued in September, and the period P&L attributes revenue by `data_emissione` (slice 4 §7.1), so a month-by-month view is skewed every time. Does the report switch to the accrual period? | No. Revenue by emission date stays the default reading, and the fiscal reports (`economic_overview`, `fiscal_estimate`) never take another. A second reading exists beside it: `GET /api/analytics/pnl?base=competenza` and `get_period_pnl(base="competenza")` attribute each invoice to `coalesce(competenza_da, data_emissione)`, where `competenza_da`/`competenza_a` is the accrual period the document declares at header level, frozen at issue and written on every `DettaglioLinee` of the XML (ORB-61). | Two readings of one figure, named, rather than one figure whose meaning changed under everyone: §7.1 chose emission on purpose and the fiscal reality is by emission or by cash. Costs and hours are attributed by their own date under both readings; only the invoice moves, and only when it declared a period. A proforma is not revenue under either. |
| 2026-09-09 | The pre-check accepts what the writer serialises: which checks were still missing from it, and where does a foreign customer's fiscal identity go? | Two. The character set, `_LATIN_RE`, now runs in `check_party_exportable` with the writer's own message and tag, through one `_latin_message` both call. And a recipient needs an `IdFiscaleIVA` or a `CodiceFiscale`: `check_recipient_identity` refuses one with neither, naming `partita_iva`, because that is where a foreign register's number belongs. `CodiceFiscale` stays the Italian register's code and a foreign identifier is never written into it. | The rule from ORB-38 holds for every constraint the writer applies, not only for the address, and `issue` calls each check before it touches the counter. The identity rule is not in the schema: `IdFiscaleIVA` and `CodiceFiscale` are both `minOccurs="0"` in `DatiAnagraficiCessionarioType`, so the document validates and the SdI rejects it with control 00417 of the Elenco dei controlli, which is why the exporter has to know it. A refusal before the number is spent costs one correction; after, it costs a hole in the register (ORB-56). |
| 2026-09-09 | A document's address line: assembled in the template, or computed in the code? | Computed. `invoices/pdf.py::indirizzo_display` builds it and both invoice templates print `{{cliente.indirizzo_display}}`. Italy keeps `CAP Comune (PR)`; outside it the postcode is the one the record holds and the province is not printed. A record with no province loses the parentheses instead of printing them empty. | The template has no punctuation that depends on a value being present. `({{cliente.provincia}})` was hard-coded, so a London customer read `EC1V 9HL London () GB` on the PDF (ORB-55), and the same hole was open on an Italian record with no province yet. Every other value these templates read is already a string `build_scope` formatted, and the PDF prints the stored postcode while the XML carries the SdI's `00000`: two audiences, one record, and the difference belongs in code that can explain itself. |
| 2026-09-10 | The history carried real clients, a real fiscal identity, a signature and two third parties' contact details (ORB-46..52). It is rewritten with `git filter-repo`, but 33 `refs/pull/N/head` on the origin still pin every pre-rewrite commit, and neither a force-push nor any API call can remove them. Publish anyway? | No. The purged history is pushed to a **new** repository of the same name and that one is made public; the old repository is renamed `orbiters-archive` and stays private, with its pull requests and its pre-rewrite objects inside it. Both histories are kept as bundles outside any repository (`~/backups`, 0600). | A repository that has ever carried unpurged history is never the one that gets published: a rewrite plus a force-push leaves the old objects reachable through refs nobody can delete, and asking GitHub Support to purge them is a request, not a guarantee. Going public is therefore a fresh remote, not a visibility flip, and the archive is deleted only when somebody decides the pull request threads are no longer worth keeping. |
| 2026-09-10 | Under which licence does the repository go public? | GNU AGPL v3 (`LICENSE`), Lorenzo's call on 2026-09-10. | Self-hosting is free and a modified version offered as a network service owes its source to its users, which is the shape of a self-hostable product that is also sold as a service. `CONTRIBUTING.md` keeps the licence among the decisions that are not an agent's to take. |
| 2026-09-10 | The repository went public on 2026-09-10 and hosted Actions minutes are free now, so the reason the trunk tier gave up "the trunk proves the whole tree on every commit" (2026-09-09 row) no longer holds. Go back to running every project on every push? | No: the scoping on `push` stays, because a fifteen-minute trunk run on a docs-only commit is still a cost, just in waiting rather than in money. `ci.yml` instead gains a nightly `schedule` run on `main` (`17 3 * * *`, UTC; first fires 2026-09-11 03:17 UTC) that proves the whole tree without putting it on the critical path of a merge (ORB-85). | The nightly run costs no new mechanism: a `schedule` event carries no `before` at all, so it earns `all=true` through the same "no usable base commit" branch a force push or a release tag already take, rather than a fourth branch written for it. It also cannot start a deploy: `deploy-*.yml`'s `workflow_run` guard already requires `github.event.workflow_run.event == 'push'`, and a scheduled run's own event is `schedule`, so the guard excludes it with no change to the deploy files. `ci` stays the one aggregate status check either way, since the schedule adds no job and touches neither its `needs` list nor its name. |
| 2026-09-10 | Same day, hours later: `orbiters-archive` still held every pre-rewrite object and the 33 pull request threads, privately and with no end date (ORB-83). Keep it as the readable record of the first month? | No, deleted (`DELETE /repos/joinorbiters/orbiters-archive`, 204, the URL answers 404). The pre-rewrite history and the pull request threads survive only as `~/backups/orbiters-pre-rewrite-*.bundle` (1,242 commits, `git bundle verify` complete) and `~/backups/orbiters-github-*.json`, both 0600 on the devbox. This supersedes the "the archive is deleted only when somebody decides" clause of the row above. | A private repository kept for one reason, holding real people's data, is the kind of thing still there in two years. What replaces it is a file nobody browses by accident and nobody can be added to, and the price is that a link to an old pull request is a 404 rather than a page. |
| 2026-09-10 | The repository is public and `main` had no protection of any kind (ORB-44). What is enforced, and who may bypass it? | Two rulesets, both `active`, no bypass actors. "main: pull request and green ci" refuses a direct push, a force-push and a deletion of `main`, and requires a pull request whose `ci` context is green, with **zero** required approvals. "release tags are immutable" refuses a deletion or a non-fast-forward on any tag. | The approval count is zero on purpose: two people merge their own work here with agents, and a review requirement neither of us can satisfy alone would be bypassed within a day. What is worth enforcing is the two things a hurry actually breaks, a merge with red CI and a rewritten tag. An emergency edits the ruleset, in public, instead of using a private bypass nobody sees. |
| 2026-09-10 | The hub's admin area and its two public wizards share one `tokens.css`, copied from the CRM's application shapes (`--radius: 10px`, a blurred `--shadow-lg`), while `joinorbiters.com`'s own pages draw no radius, a solid 2px ink border and a stepped offset shadow instead (`landing.css:9`, `landing.css:31`). The chooser, the two wizards and the thanks page are reached mid-sentence from the landing's own call to action (ORB-73): which system do they follow? | The site's. `projects/hub/apps/web/src/styles/tokens.css` gains a `.site` scope restating the landing's own values -- `--radius: 0`, a solid ink `--border`/`--input`, an 8px/6px offset `--shadow-xs`..`--shadow-lg`, and the `--landing-*` colours, which already equal the application's own `--primary`/`--ring` -- for whichever element carries that class. The admin area is untouched: it is the tool, not a page the landing links to, and keeps the application's rounded tokens exactly as they stood. | A public page added to the hub reaches for the `.site` class and the `--landing-*` tokens instead of typing a new radius or a soft shadow; the admin area's shapes cannot drift, since nothing there carries the class. `tokens.test.ts` pins the rule mechanically: no `--radius` above 0 and no blur term in any `--shadow-*` inside `.site`. ORB-74 (page chrome) and ORB-75 (fields and buttons) apply the class and the component shapes; this decision changes no markup. |
| 2026-09-10 | Ivan's issued invoices are the previous system's PDFs, kept at import; a proforma rendered by PigroCRM sat beside them looking like another product («la proforma non ha il template uguale a quella delle fatture», ORB-102). Whose layout does the invoice PDF follow? | The inherited register's, for the fattura and the proforma alike: a full emitter block top right (name, `Identificativo fiscale ai fini IVA`, codice fiscale, `Regime fiscale: RF19 Regime forfettario`, codice destinatario, address, email), a `TD01 fattura \| Numero \| Data` line, the customer on two bar-separated lines, a three-column detail table with the Natura in `%IVA` when there is no rate, the payment code with its words (`MP05`, `Bonifico IBAN …`), a rule, a «Thank you!» with the contacts, `1.760,00 €`, `dd-mm-yyyy`, a page number and nothing else in the footer. One template (`template-invoice.md`) for both documents, the proforma declaration under a condition; the invoice has its own page header (`header-invoice.typ.template`), offers and time reports keep `header.typ.template`. What survives the mapping and is still outside `[\x00-\xff]` (a currency sign, a letter from another script, an emoji) is refused with the field named, and `issue` now runs that check on the document's own text (`Causale`, `Descrizione`, `UnitaMisura`, `RiferimentoNormativo`) through `check_document_text_exportable`, before the counter moves. A refusal over how a person types a dash is a refusal over spelling, and a spelling the schema cannot carry is spelled, not refused; a refusal over content is real and comes before the number is spent. The mapping is punctuation and spaces only, so a euro sign in a PEC still refuses as ORB-56 pinned it. |

| 2026-09-22 | A NixOS workstation has none of `uv`, `pnpm`, `node`, `python3`, `pandoc` or `typst` on its PATH, so the README's "Getting started" does not start there; and the repository is public under a licence whose point is that self-hosting is free. Does the repository carry a `flake.nix`, and what does it build? | Yes, on `flake-parts`, in one file: a `devShells.default`; the five deployables as packages, read from the two locks the images install from (uv2nix over `uv.lock`, `pnpm.fetchDeps` over `pnpm-lock.yaml`); one NixOS module per product (`services.pigrocrm`, `services.rebase-hub`, `services.rebase-website`) restating its compose file and its `deploy/` nginx configuration; and one VM test per module, booting it and probing it through nginx. A compose service is a systemd unit, so each API module also runs its MCP server (ORB-170, REB-213) behind the same `/mcp` locations, and the CRM boots as its image does: the root migration, `ensure-space-defaults` (ORB-189), then uvicorn. The security headers production adds at the host's vhost (REB-275) come from one keyed `nginx-headers` module the three import, added once per name however many share it. No image: the compose stacks stay CI's to build. Every version in it is read from the file that already pins it (`.python-version`, `package.json`'s `engines`, the `ARG`s of `projects/pigrocrm/Dockerfile.api`); the literals are hashes. Pandoc and Typst are the upstream release tarballs at the Dockerfile's exact versions, each asserting its own `--version` at build time, because a fixed-output derivation's hash alone lets a stale tarball build a new version name on a machine that already holds it. Measured before deciding on the exact pin: the five renderer test files pass on nixpkgs' typst 0.15.1 and pandoc 3.7.0.2 today (104 of 105, the one failure being `strings` absent from the probe shell), so it buys "a local green proves what CI proves" for the one component the repository documents version-specific workarounds for, not a passing suite. The Nix build and the VM tests run on the local tier only (`nix-packages`, `nix-modules` in `preflight.json`), not on hosted CI, and only on the files the flake itself reads (the locks, the manifests, the Dockerfiles, compose, `deploy/`, the migrations): what they prove is packaging, and a source change is already proved by the builds and suites the other checks run. Opened as PR 65 on 2026-09-11 and landed after the rename to rebase (REB-204): every identifier in it is the renamed one. | The modules are how a third party runs the software on NixOS. They are not a release path for rebase's own environments, which the 2026-09-09 row keeps on compose and `_deploy-compose.yml`: a preview or a production of ours is never brought up from `nixosModules`. A version the repository pins lives in one file and the flake reads it. Where a module restates a compose file or a `deploy/` nginx configuration, the VM test is what keeps the copy true, and a change to either file selects that test in preflight. A `pnpm-lock.yaml` change moves one hash in `flake.nix`, and the local tier is where that is found. Docker and Playwright's browsers stay the host's. |

| 2026-09-25 | A Google consent that outlasts what is left of the fifteen-minute access cookie came back to a callback that answered `{"detail":"Autenticazione richiesta"}` (production, space `rebase-demo`, REB-446): the callback is a top-level navigation, so the SPA never renews the session before it. How does the Gmail or Drive callback know whose browser came back, and what does it say when nobody's did? | Through `deps.callback_actor`: `get_actor` first, and when it refuses the cookie, the refresh cookie of the same prefix, accepted when `POST /api/auth/refresh` would accept it (signature, type, a row in this database for its user that is unexpired and unconsumed or rotated away inside the ten-second grace, the user still active; a token consumed long ago is refused without being judged a replay) and neither rotated nor answered with any cookie; the SPA renews the pair on landing, as after any idle tab. The `state` is still redeemed against that actor's own id, in the database of the prefix the request wore. With no live session the callback redirects into the SPA with `esito=sessione` (Gmail: «Primi passi», whatever the space holds; Drive: its settings page), which reads «La sessione è scaduta mentre eri su Google» and offers «Riprova». A session opened by a signup alone has no refresh cookie until the welcome link proves the address, so it is not rescued and lands on `sessione` too, on purpose. | The consent's way back never ends on a 401 document. A navigation that needs the actor may read the refresh cookie as proof of who the browser is, and never rotates or mints from it: a rotation there would have to carry its new pair on every answer, a 403 problem document included, or leave a consumed token in the browser that the next refresh reads as a replay; a mint without rotation renews a session past the replay check. The `state` alone is never the credential: it is an unsigned value that travels in URLs, and trusting it would let anyone who read a consent URL pair it with a code from their own Google account (PKCE does not help, the challenge travels in the same URL) and connect their mailbox to somebody else's user. |

| 2026-09-25 | A campaign send retries: the tick that dies after Resend accepted a mail, or one Resend answers `429`/`5xx`/a concurrent-idempotency `409` to, runs the same row again. The loop retries within the minute, so only a `campaigns` service down for more than a day, right after a tick died between Resend's answer and its own commit, could send that address a second mail; nothing reconciles that case today. `disiscrizione_token` is never hashed at rest, unlike every other credential this schema stores (`admin_tokens.token_hash`, `magic_link_tokens.token_hash`), and what a leaked token can do is only unsubscribe that one address. |

| 2026-10-05 | Lorenzo wants a password manager available to every Rebase member (REB-644): open source, running on a server of ours, and an admin shares credentials per collection or group so that each member sees only what they were given. The permission model is free in it: an organisation holds collections, each collection is assigned to a named member with one of view, view with hidden passwords, edit, edit with hidden passwords, or manage, and members join by emailed invitation with open signups off. Groups exist but behind `ORG_GROUPS_ENABLED`, which the project's own template calls a beta feature with known issues, so the requirement is met by collections and not by groups. Rejected: Bitwarden's own server, because a self-hosted organisation needs an Enterprise licence (US$6 per user per month, billed annually) or a Families one (six users at most, no groups), and neither fits every member with per-collection access; groups need Teams or Enterprise, and its Lite image is documented as not for business. Runner-up: Passbolt CE (AGPL-3.0, published Cure53 and Quarkslab audits), which loses on three counts: a lost passphrase means deleting the user and losing whatever only they owned (account recovery, SSO and the activity log are Pro, EUR 4.5 per user per month, minimum 10 users); an administrator who is not the group's manager cannot add anyone to it, and nobody can grant a secret they do not hold, because sharing re-encrypts each secret for the newcomer; and a new member needs the browser extension, a passphrase and a recovery kit before first use. Also looked at and left out: Infisical, a platform for secrets, certificates and privileged access for machines and pipelines, not a vault for people's logins, with `ee/` held under a separate licence; Psono CE, which keeps SSO and audit logging in its Enterprise Edition. Sources are in REB-644. Lorenzo's call on 2026-10-05: Vaultwarden, for the team and the freelancers with an active match, not for every signup; the other members are added when there is a reason to. Running it costs no licence fee: it is one container on a host we already pay for, and the only new costs are the SMTP provider for invitations and the off-host backup storage, both settled in REB-648. Organisation owners and admins can open every collection whether or not it is assigned to them, so the per-collection permissions bind ordinary members only, and the owner and admin roles are kept to the few people who are trusted with all of it. | Nothing earlier than Vaultwarden 1.37.0 is deployed, since the eleven advisories of 2026-10-03, among them organisation import, cross-organisation cipher access and collection links, are all fixed there. It is never deployed by hand, through CI like every other stack (2026-09-09 rows); the follow-up card decides the trigger and the environments. Access is granted by collection to named members, and `ORG_GROUPS_ENABLED` stays off until the project stops calling it beta. Vaultwarden has had 26 security advisories published in 2026, at least 12 of them on organisation, collection, group or cipher access, so it is upgraded within days of a release that carries a security fix, and nothing is stored in it before the data directory has a tested off-host backup. A lost master password is recoverable only for a member enrolled in the account recovery policy, which needs SMTP; one who is not loses their personal items, so the invitation tells members that. The pick changes to Passbolt CE if advisories on the authorisation code keep arriving at the 2026 rate after 1.37, or if one of them exposes plaintext instead of ciphertext and metadata; it changes to Bitwarden Enterprise, the same clients with a vendor behind them, if Rebase decides to pay per seat. |
| 2026-10-05 | A referral's attribution is taken from a public `rif` code and the email it is posted with, and the first writer wins an address's attribution (REB-646, split from REB-566). Someone can post a prospect's email with an accomplice's code, and the real person can then never change it. An admin sees who referred whom and the amounts, but not the code used, when the signup happened, or whether the referred person ever logged in. How does an attribution prove the referred person's identity? | Two steps, in this order. First, the ledger row shows the evidence (the code used, the signup time, whether the email domains match, whether the referred user ever logged in, the UTM source), and nothing about the public routes changes (REB-657). Second, a referral is recorded as pending at signup and counts only once the referred person is verified; REB-658 picks the event that verifies them, a first login or a first signed letter. Rejected for now: replacing an unverified attribution with the code the person presents on their own first login, because it changes who owns an attribution; and blocking self-dealing at signup by normalised email, domain or LinkedIn, because it is easy to evade and is only worth building on top of the evidence. Decided by Lorenzo on 2026-10-05, taking the recommendation of the analysis on REB-646. | A referral reaches the ledger as confirmable only after the referred identity is verified, once REB-658 lands; until then an admin confirms it against the evidence shown on the row, never on the names alone. A new entry point for attribution (REB-554, REB-600) records the same evidence and the same pending state. |

Turning any of them on later is a product change with its own card, never an inherited default. | What an agent receives from the MCP server is decided here, not by the SDK: the options object leaves no surface-shaping default implicit, and `test_analytics_instrumentation.py` holds `tools/list` and a tool's result equal between the server a key instruments and the one it does not, so an SDK upgrade that moves a default fails the suite instead of shipping. | 2026-10-05 | Every settings save in PigroCRM is a whole-row `PUT` with no version, so two admins editing the same row at once had the second Salva overwrite the first's fields in silence; the SPA's reseed of an untouched form covered the common case and not a draft started before the other save (CodeRabbit on PR #506, REB-622). How is a stale settings save refused, and where is that decided? | Once, for every whole-row settings `PUT`: the read carries `updated_at`, the body sends the one the draft was built on, and `core/versioning.py`'s `require_unchanged` refuses a mismatch with `StaleRow` (`stale_row`, 409) before anything is assigned, under the row's lock (`refresh(with_for_update=True)`, an advisory lock where there is no row). A key that was sent is checked, `null` included; a body without the key checks nothing, so the MCP tools and an older client keep the whole-row replace. The panel shows «Qualcun altro ha salvato nel frattempo.» with «Ricarica», which takes the other admin's row under the fields the person touched. | A new whole-row settings `PUT` takes `updated_at`, checks it whenever it was sent and calls `require_unchanged` under a lock; it never compares timestamps its own way, never turns the check into ordering, and never makes the version a column the write assigns. A field-level `PATCH` is not a whole-row save and does not take it. Spec 2026-10-03 §11, 2026-10-05. |
| 2026-10-05 | A referral is taken from a public `rif` code and the email it is posted with, and the first writer wins an address's attribution (Lorenzo's call on 2026-10-05, REB-646), so someone can post a prospect's email with an accomplice's code. REB-646 decided that a referral counts only once the referred person is verified and left REB-658 to pick the event that verifies them: the first magic-link login, or the first signed letter. What does the system know at each moment, and which of the two can an attacker holding only the prospect's email trigger? | The first magic-link login, taken after the referral was recorded, for a referred freelancer and a referred company alike; and, for a referred freelancer only, their own signature of a letter, as a second door to the same proof. This is the choice REB-658 was asked to make, not part of Lorenzo's call. `Referral` carries `stato` (`da_verificare` until proved, then `verificato`), `verified_at` and `verified_via` (`accesso`, `lettera`, or `storico` for a row migration 0030 found already counting with no earlier proof). Why the login: a `Login` row exists only when `UserService.enter` spends a one-time token mailed to the referred address, so it needs the mailbox, and an attacker who knows the address can ask for the mail (`/auth/link`, or a repeated `apply`) but cannot open it. Why only a login made at or after the referral: an address that already logged in before a squatter posted it has proved nothing about the squatter's claim, so an older login never counts. This stops a past login from counting, not a future routine one: a member who logs in next week verifies what was posted for their address meanwhile, which is the residual below. Why the letter only for a freelancer: `SigningService._dispatch` sends the Documenso envelope to the freelancer's address and the freelancer is the only signer, so their signature proves the same mailbox; a company is never a signer, and the first signed letter of a match says nothing about its referente, so a company is verified by a login and never by a letter. A pending referral has no `ReferralReward`: `record_reward_if_signed` writes none, and writes it at the later of the first signed letter and the verification (`verify_on_login` writes it when a company's referente logs in after the letter was signed). The ledger and the «Match» list show it as `Da verificare` with no confirm button and, for a company, no match and no estimate. The public routes answer the same for a new address, an address already attributed and one already holding a card, and a repeat post still never replaces the attribution. Rejected: the first signed letter alone (it verifies no company, and an attribution on a company would pay out only after an engagement had been bought, with nobody having proved who the referente is); a login at any time (it verifies a squatted address of a person who was already a member). | A new entry point for attribution (REB-554, REB-600) calls `link_signup`, which records the referral pending, and gets the same verification for free. Not solved, on purpose: the real person logging in verifies the *squatter's* attribution, since the first writer wins an address (REB-646), so the proof is of the person and not of the code, and an admin still reads the evidence on the row (REB-657) before confirming; replacing an attribution with the code the person presents, and self-dealing detection, stay out (REB-646). Migration 0030 sets an existing row `verificato` when it already has a reward (`storico`), a freelancer's signed letter (`lettera`) or a login at or after it (`accesso`), and `da_verificare` otherwise, so nothing that counted stops counting. |
| 2026-10-05 | `_deploy-compose.yml` «Configure SSH» ran `ssh-keyscan -H "$DEPLOY_HOST" >> ~/.ssh/known_hosts` on every deploy and trusted whatever key answered: trust on first use, on a connection that carries the repository, the compose commands and, since REB-650, a secret written into the host `.env`. `DEPLOY_SSH_KEY` proved the runner to the host; nothing proved the host to the runner (flagged on REB-650, CWE-295). How does the deploy know it is talking to the host? | By a pinned key: a fifth Environment secret, `DEPLOY_KNOWN_HOSTS`, the host's `known_hosts` line as `ssh-keyscan -H -t ed25519 "$DEPLOY_HOST"` prints it, captured once by a person who confirms the fingerprint on the host itself (`ssh-keygen -lf /etc/ssh/ssh_host_ed25519_key.pub`), written to `~/.ssh/known_hosts` by the deploy and checked by every `ssh` and `rsync` with `StrictHostKeyChecking=yes`, that file as the only `UserKnownHostsFile` and `GlobalKnownHostsFile=/dev/null`, so nothing but the pin can vouch for the host. The deploy never runs `ssh-keyscan`, and the person compares the fingerprint of the very line they store, not of a second scan. | A deploy to a host whose key is not the pinned one fails before anything is sent. A hashed line matches only the exact string it was scanned with, so the value is scanned against the environment's own `DEPLOY_HOST` and the «Configure SSH» step fails loudly when it does not name it. The key is rotated only when the host's key really changes, with a new value captured the same way, never with a looser check. Every `<name>-preview` and `<name>-production` carries the secret; the procedure is `docs/adding-a-project.md` § 7. Supersedes the «same four secrets» clause of the two 2026-09-09 rows above (where the configuration lives, and no release by hand); the rest of both stands (REB-653). |
| 2026-10-06 | REB-658 records a referral as `da_verificare` until the referred person logs in (or, for a freelancer, signs a letter), but the framework agreement and the letter printed `Segnalato da` for a pending one too, which can be squatted (REB-646). Does a contract name a referrer nobody has proved? | No: `ReferralService.referrer_name`, the one source `_quadro_data` and `_lettera_data` read, returns a name only for a `verificato` referral; a pending one prints `nessuno`. Lorenzo's call on 2026-10-06 (REB-664). | A signed document never states an attribution nobody has proved. No surface computes the line itself. The framework agreement is written before the freelancer's first letter signature, so a freelancer who has not logged in yet gets `nessuno` on it; the agreement is rebuilt when it is sent for signature (`data_for_sending`), so a login before dispatch prints the name, while a letter keeps the `azienda-segnalata-da` it stored at creation. |

Where does an ignore live? | In the root `.snyk`, as an `ignore` entry per Snyk id with path `'*'`, its `reason` and an `expires` date a month ahead, and `snyk-weekly.yml` passes that file to the three container steps with `--policy-path` (and to no other command). An image finding ignored only in the UI comes back on the next Monday. `tooling/snyk-weekly/README.md` § Where an ignore lives holds the procedure. |

| 2026-10-06 | The Vaultwarden pick (2026-10-05, REB-644) left the deployment open (REB-648): which host, which database, which mail, which backup, how is it released, and how are the admin page and signups kept shut? | Host: the production host that already runs the site, the hub, the CRM and Documenso (Lorenzo, 2026-10-06), in a compose project of its own, `vaultwarden`, at `projects/vaultwarden`, on `vault.letsrebase.com`, loopback port 8091; a second host was not needed for one container and a dozen members. Image: `vaultwarden/server:1.37.4` by tag and digest. Mail: Resend's SMTP endpoint (`smtp.resend.com`, 587, STARTTLS) with a Resend API key made for Vaultwarden alone and the sender `vault@letsrebase.com`; the hub mails through Resend's HTTP API, which this server cannot speak, so the key is a new one and Lorenzo confirms it (his 2026-10-05 call, "reuse the hub's mail", assumed the hub sent over SMTP). Backup: once a day, encrypted to a public certificate on the host (the private key is on no server) into a private Cloudflare R2 bucket in Terraform, ninety days of objects, spend approved by Lorenzo, 2026-10-05; the restore on a scratch container is the proof and `tests/backup-restore.sh` repeats it in CI. Signups: `SIGNUPS_ALLOWED` is a literal false in the compose file, members join by invitation, and only the owner's address may create an organisation (`ORG_CREATION_USERS`); the admin page is off while `ADMIN_TOKEN` is unset and, when opened for the first account, takes an Argon2id hash. | Everything about the instance is configured in the repository: the admin page is closed after the first account, because a setting saved there goes to `config.json` and overrides the compose file. A secret the compose file needs is a GitHub Environment secret listed in `env-secrets` (2026-10-05, REB-650); the host `.env` holds only the data directory, port, public name, sender, the owner's address, the account id and the backup's public certificate. Moving the instance to another host is the Environment's `DEPLOY_HOST`, the DNS record and a restore from the backup, nothing else. Nothing is stored in it before `docs/restore.md` has been run once on a scratch container against the real bucket (2026-10-05 row). Supersedes the clause of the 2026-10-05 Vaultwarden row that left "the trigger and the environments" to the follow-up card: the trigger is the tag and the environment is production alone; the rest of that row stands. |

The cost: whoever restores a contract loads the key that also opens the password manager's backup, and a new bucket with its own key pair is the cleaner end state once a Cloudflare token with R2 edit is to hand (`DOCUMENSO_BACKUP_BUCKET` and two Environment secrets change, no code). Until it exists, the bucket, its key pair and the `documenso/` rule are shared with Vaultwarden's. The secrets the dump leaves out have to be kept by a person (the `Infrastructure` collection of the password manager), or a recovery on a new host cannot seal or fully read what the database holds. |

| 2026-10-26 | Slice 7 (§8) left push and reminder email out of the CRM, on the ground that mailing on someone's behalf is what slice 5 chose never to automate. PigroCRM raises reminders for what falls due (attività, deals, invoices, contracts, days, the space's own health), shows them in the app, and delivers them by push, Slack and email. Recipients are the assignee plus the owner of the linked customer or deal, and admins alone for space-wide ones. Every timing, threshold, channel, urgency and quiet hours is configurable at two levels, the admins' space defaults and each user's own override. Delivery defaults to push and Slack at once, email at once only for urgent reminders and one daily summary for the rest. The catalogue, the defaults (which are Lorenzo's and which are proposals) and how a pass runs across one database per space are in `projects/pigrocrm/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-26-promemoria-design.md`, pending his sign-off. | A reminder is a notice to a person of the space, never a mail to a customer: `payment_reminders` (the sollecito) is still the act and stays as slice 7 §9 says. Buttons to complete or snooze in Slack are a later card. A webhook would post reminders that are personal to a channel that reaches the wrong people. | Slack delivery is per user by direct message, recorded in the delivery ledger like email and push. The bot token is a per-space secret and never travels in a log or an MCP answer. REB-739 connects a workspace, REB-740 delivers. |

Both tools stay on the default MCP surface: archive is a soft delete and `restore_attivita` undoes it, which is what the spec's own reasoning protects. §7 and §10.7 are amended with a dated note; `delete_attivita` stays unregistered and nothing hard-deletes an attività. | An MCP tool over an attività may archive and restore it and may never delete it for good. Closing as `annullata` stays the usual way to drop a task, and archive is for the one entered by mistake. |

| 2026-10-26 | The contract page (REB-741, REB-742) needed two moves the API did not have: changing a contract's `stato`, and replacing the rate in force with a newer one, which the database's non-overlap rule refuses. How are they allowed? | A contract's state is its own act, `POST /api/contracts/{id}/state` (MCP `set_contract_state`), from any of `bozza`, `attivo`, `terminato`, `scaduto` to any other and never to the one it has, and each move is one row in its timeline; `PATCH /api/contracts/{id}` cannot flip it. A new rate replaces the old one only when the caller says so: `chiudi_precedente` on the create call ends, the day before the new start, every card that began earlier and is still in force on that day, in the same transaction, and without it an overlap stays the database's 409. The flag is an argument of the call, not a field of `RateCardCreate`, because a proposal carries that schema for its first card. Nothing moves a contract to `scaduto` on a timer, as in mastro. | No screen or agent writes `Contract.stato` through an edit, and no rate is ever closed implicitly: closing the previous card is a choice the person makes on the form and the agent makes with the flag. An expense already on an invoice has no edit on the contract page, since its amount is on a document by then. |
| 2026-10-26 | PigroCRM can be installed as an app (REB-731), and push (REB-738) will need the same worker. What may that worker keep, and what is one installed app? | The worker keeps nothing a person is shown: Cache Storage holds the offline page and the files it is drawn with, all written by the build before any session exists, and the worker answers a page load with that page (status 503) only when the network is gone. An installed app is one space: its own manifest and worker at `/<slug>/app/`, scope `/<slug>/app` with no trailing slash. | No document and no API response is ever stored by the worker, so there is no stale figure to pass off as current and no other person's data to serve on a shared device: the rule that keeps this true is in `sw/index.ts`, and `sw/policy.ts` `decideFetch` is where a request is told apart. A feature that wants to cache more (an offline read, a queue of writes) is a decision of its own, not a line in the worker. The scope loses its trailing slash because the router's home is `/<slug>/app?tab=...`, which `/<slug>/app/` would leave outside the app; nginx therefore sends `Service-Worker-Allowed` for the worker (`projects/pigrocrm/deploy/nginx/spa.conf`). |
| 2026-10-26 | The invoices page has to import FatturaPA files (REB-758), but `review_invoice_import` and `confirm_invoice_import` take the id of an archived document, and a document needs an owner: the customer the invoice is billed to, who for a new counterparty does not exist until the confirmation creates it. What does the page send? | The files themselves, twice and in batches. `POST /api/invoices/import/upload/review` reads them and writes nothing; `POST /api/invoices/import/upload/confirm` gets the same files again with one decision per customer (create it, or attach this one), keyed by the `party_key` the review names (the normalised Italian tax id, else `estero:` and the identifier the document states, else the name), and re-reads them from their own bytes. A ZIP is opened on the server, one level deep, bounded in files, bytes per file and bytes in total. A file that looks like a FatturaPA and does not parse is one unclaimed row with its reason, not a refusal of the batch. The confirmation runs the same `_confirm_content` as `confirm_import`, and a single-invoice file's XML is archived as a `fattura_xml` document of the invoice's customer inside that invoice's transaction; a `lotto` file archives nothing, as for `confirm_import`. | Nothing is stored between the review and the write, and a file is archived only under the customer it was written for. `review_upload` and `confirm_upload` are bytes the MCP never carries (the sweep lists them with the other bytes). The host nginx vhosts need the 105m block for `/api/invoices/import/upload` applied by hand, as the documents block was. |

The year's CSV is one file per azienda in Italian spreadsheet format: UTF-8 with a byte-order mark, semicolons, decimal commas, the invoice number and the partita IVA as text cells, one row per invoice line and then the register, each ending in a total that equals the year's revenue figure. | A figure the accountant opens in Excel is written for the locale that opens it, not for the one a terminal reads well, and free text in it is defused against formula injection. |
| 2026-10-26 | Impostazioni grew to thirteen flat tabs and four more sections are coming (reminder defaults, Notifiche, Slack, Esecuzioni); how is it grouped, who sees what, and what keeps old links working (REB-770, implemented by REB-771)? | Six groups, seventeen sections, no URL moves. Personale (Profilo, Notifiche), Spazio e persone (Spazio, Utenti), Clienti e deal (Campi, Pipeline, Template), Fatturazione e costi (Aziende, Categorie costo, Tariffe, Periodi), Collegamenti (Gmail, Google Drive, Slack), Promemoria e automazioni (Promemoria, Automazioni, Esecuzioni); the four new sections are slots for their own cards (REB-729, REB-735, REB-739, REB-764). Option A: the sidebar's Impostazioni lists the six groups, each linking to its first section the reader may open, and the page's tab strip lists only the sections of the current group. R1 one list (`SETTINGS_GROUPS`) feeds the page, the sidebar and `canSeeSettingsTab`; R2 the Impostazioni entry is shown to every role, each role seeing only the groups with a section it may open (collaboratore: Personale and Collegamenti, readonly: Personale); R3 the URL is the section id `/app/settings/<id>` and never carries the group, and every existing path, the Gmail and Drive consent redirects included, keeps answering; R4 `/app/settings/profile?da=digest` redirects to Notifiche, forwarding the query string, once Notifiche exists, and stays on Profilo until then; R5 bare `/app/settings` opens Profilo, the first section every role can open; R6 admin-only is a property of the section, not of the group; R7 Slack is shown to the admin in its own section and to each person as one read-only line in Notifiche; R8 a stale job raises a reminder, it adds no settings state. | A component never writes a section label or a group by hand: sections and groups come from `SETTINGS_GROUPS` (`features/settings/tabs.ts`), a path never carries a group, a group a role cannot open is absent and never greyed, and the tab strip never lists a section outside the current group. |

| 2026-10-27 | How do email and web push (REB-737, REB-738) leave the reminder pass, and what do they promise? | Channels plug into the pass after the reads and before the ledger writes (`reminders/dispatch.py`): a channel is handed what the person has not acknowledged or snoozed, plans from plain values, sends with no transaction open and never raises (the dispatcher guards the one that does), and the pass writes what the provider accepted in its one transaction, so a failed send leaves nothing and the next pass sends again. Push: a notification per reminder to every browser the person subscribed from, signed with one VAPID private key per environment (`PIGROCRM_VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY`; the public half is derived from it), through `pywebpush`, held a day by the push service when the browser is off. A browser announces what reached the list after it subscribed and within the last day, never the backlog. A subscription belongs to the person who last subscribed from that browser, starts over when it changes hands, goes when its person signs out, and is deleted when the push service reports it gone (404, 410); a person keeps ten at most. The endpoint is a URL the server calls, so only the browser vendors' own push services are accepted when it is stored, and it must resolve to public addresses, with no redirect followed, when called; a pass gives the push phase two minutes. | A new channel is one `Channel` in `reminders/channels.py` and never edits the pass. The summary hour and the quiet hours stay preferences in Impostazioni (summary hour 8, quiet hours 20:00 to 08:00 and the weekend by default), never host settings; the host holds only the VAPID key and subject. The first pass after the Resend key is configured mails every urgent reminder open at that moment, which `pigrocrm reminders --dry-run` counts beforehand. The MCP surface does not follow push: a subscription is something only a browser can produce. |

(REB-879) | A thread, as the hub's (REB-856): the table `notes` (`entity_type` deal, customer or person, `entity_id`, `testo`, `autore_id` and `autore` the name as it read then, `fonte` `web`, `mcp`, `sistema` or `rebase` derived from the `Actor` and never passed by a caller, `created_at`, `modificata_at`/`modificata_da_id`, soft delete) replaces the three `note` columns (migration 0064 moved every non-blank one in as the record's first note, with no author and no source, shown «Nota precedente», dated by the row's `updated_at`). A machine key a door must find a record by is a column no note text can carry or erase, never a line of a note. Over MCP the thread is served by three tools, `add_note` (exactly one of `deal_id`, `customer_id`, `person_id`), `update_note` and `delete_note`, none taking an author or a date; the default tool list's budget moves from 90,000 to 91,500 bytes for these three tools (supersedes the 90,000 of the 2026-10-27 row; the full-access one stays 105,000). |

(REB-894, REB-895) | The API images run as uid 10001 on a read-only root, the web containers as `nginx` (nginx-unprivileged, port 8080 inside), all with `cap_drop: ALL`, `no-new-privileges`, rotated logs, memory ceilings and a health check; Postgres keeps only the five capabilities its entrypoint needs. The base images stay on a tag, not a digest, because `build --pull` is how Debian and Alpine patches arrive (REB-462); the database images are pinned by tag and digest in all three compose files and the backup Dockerfile, and a bump is a reviewed pull request. |
| 2026-11-02 | The CRM migrations ran in the API's start-up command, so boot time and downtime grew with the number of spaces and the old API was already down meanwhile. Where do they run? (INFRA-A-10) | In a one-shot `migrate` service that `api` waits for (`service_completed_successfully`): `alembic upgrade head`, then `ensure-space-defaults`, as the owner role. It also hands the documents bind mount to uid 10001 as root and then drops privileges, so the unprivileged API needs nothing done on the host. The previous `api` keeps serving until the new one is up, so a migration has to stay compatible with the code one release back. | `_deploy-compose.yml` accepts `exited (0)` for the service named `migrate` and for no other. |
| 2026-11-02 | The applications' rate limiters are per process, and nothing capped the public doors in front of them. Where does the real limit live, and what is limited? (INFRA-A-14) | In nginx: `infra/origin/nginx/rate-limits.conf` declares the `limit_req` zones once at the http level (production and preview share them) and the vhosts name them per location. Limited: space creation and the slug typeahead, the POSTs that issue a credential (login, link, verify, invite, the identity's enter and invite), the MCP endpoints, the hub wizard's signup, its magic-link POSTs and its member lookup. Not limited: `me`, `refresh`, `logout` and the space list (every open tab calls them continuously and the SPA reads a failed `/me` as a logged-out session), and the Resend and Documenso webhooks (a 429 only makes the sender retry). | A zone keys on `$request_method`-guarded variables so only the writes count; the over-limit status is set in the location, never at the http level. |
| 2026-11-02 | The CRM held customers' fiscal data and had only a manual unencrypted `pg_dumpall`. How is it backed up, and must production have the backup configured to deploy? (INFRA-A-07) | Every night at 03:00 UTC the hub's backup image dumps the whole server (roles, the register and every `pigro_t_*` space, listed at each run), encrypted to the same certificate as the hub's and Vaultwarden's, into the hub's bucket under `pigrocrm/`, with R2 retention locks of 30 days. It is an overlay, `docker-compose.backup.yml`, that only production's host `.env` turns on (`COMPOSE_FILE`), and its three Environment secrets are `optional-env-secrets`: the deploy writes them when they exist and does not fail when they do not. | Enable it by creating the secrets, then setting `COMPOSE_FILE`; stop any old backup cron after the first object appears. `restore-from-backup.md` is the runbook. |
