The registry never creates a database or a table: `ensure_tenants_database` is the boot's (`ensure-space-defaults`), so a process that only serves needs no owner credentials for it. A space's effective `Settings` are resolved once per TTL and kept as one object while its rows are unchanged (PAPI-04). | The API and the MCP server each hold a registry: the worst case is `2 * 12 * 4` connections plus the root's and the registry's. A request-path module never runs DDL. |
| 2026-11-02 | The four `SECURITY DEFINER` helpers of migration 0048 read past the row policies for any UUID and had `search_path = public` and `EXECUTE` for PUBLIC (PCORE-B-09, B-10). Which paths are unscoped, and how are they closed? | Those four are the only unscoped read path of the schema; migration 0066 pins `search_path = pg_catalog, public, pg_temp` and takes `EXECUTE` from PUBLIC, and `ensure_application_role` grants it back to the application role by name, which also loses `INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE` on `alembic_version`. `test_architecture.py` fails on any `text(...)`/`execute(...)` built from a value outside `db/sidecar.py` and the proforma sequence. | A fifth definer function, or SQL assembled from input, is a decision recorded here first: the policies read a setting any session may write, and hold only while no statement is built from input. |
| 2026-11-02 | A customer's `azienda_id` could be moved by anyone who may write the customer while its owner is an admin's change (PCORE-B-28). Is that asymmetry a rule? | Yes. Moving a customer to another azienda is a writer's decision and moves nothing already created under it (spec 2026-10-03 §1.7), and a scoped member's row policy refuses a move out of their own scope on both sides. The owner is different: it decides who is told about the customer's invoices and contracts (REB-773), so it stays an admin's. | The two rules are not unified. Changing who is notified is `change_customer_owner`, never a side effect of moving the customer. |
| 2026-11-02 | The registry's pool of 2 plus 2 connections per space (the row above) is too small when one request holds two connections, as a dashboard's snapshot session does; and the API and the MCP server refused nothing on a placeholder signing key. Superseding the sizes and the boot rule. | Each space engine keeps a pool of 4 with 4 more on overflow and at most 8 engines are open (64 connections at rest and 128 at the burst ceiling per process pair, the root's and the registry's on top). `create_app()` and `McpHttpApp` call `require_production_secrets`, so neither exists on the published placeholder over TLS. Migration 0065 refuses to seal under a placeholder secret and has a real downgrade, and a secret row not yet sealed is read as it is. | A request path never meets «permission denied for function»: migration 0066 grants the definer helpers to the application role in its own transaction. |
| 2026-11-02 | A personal access token is accepted on every REST route and `AGENT_FORBIDDEN_ACTIONS` was a denylist, so every operation nobody named was open to it: over REST a token could send mail from the owner's mailbox, create or promote administrators with a password, rewrite the space settings (the `mcp_full_access` ceiling included), mint further tokens and disconnect Gmail (REB-880, PAPI-01). Which operations may an agent credential perform? | Only the ones classified for it, in `core/agent_actions.py`: `AGENT_ALLOWED_ACTIONS` (what a tool does, mirroring the MCP surface), `FULL_ACCESS_ACTIONS` (the old denylist, open to a token with `full_access` under the space's ceiling, REB-810 unchanged) and `PERSON_ONLY_ACTIONS` (accounts and tokens, space settings, the connections to Google and Slack, mail that leaves in the owner's name, the shape of the CRM). `Actor` refuses an action in none of them. Action keys are English snake_case; the Italian sentence a person reads is a label in `ACTION_LABELS`. A route with no service behind it takes `person_only(action)`. | A new `require_*` action is closed to agents until it is classified; `packages/core/tests/test_agent_actions.py` fails on an unclassified or stale key, and the credential walk in `apps/api/tests/test_role_matrix.py` lists every route an administrator's token reaches, so a new reachable route is a reviewed line. Supersedes the denylist reading of the 2026-10-26 and 2026-10-27 rows above, which named `AGENT_FORBIDDEN_ACTIONS` (now `FULL_ACCESS_ACTIONS`). |
| 2026-11-02 | With a PostHog key set, the MCP adapter sent the parameters and the response of every tool call to PostHog Cloud (customer names, tax ids, notes, mail bodies), and the 2026-10-27 marker (REB-812) missed bare lists, names and the resources' own header (PMCP-01, PMCP-04). What leaves the process, and what is marked? | The adapter's `before_send` (`analytics.without_crm_content`) drops `$mcp_parameters`, `$mcp_response`, `$mcp_error_message` and the exception fields: an event keeps the tool name, duration, error flag and type, the actor and the space group. The marker is by tool as well as by key: `provenance.FREE_TEXT_TOOLS` names the tools that return records or text from outside, `FREE_TEXT_KEYS` stays as the backstop and gained `ragione_sociale`, `nome`, `cognome`, `ruolo`, `fornitore`, `testo_letterale` and `interpretazione_adottata`. A tool that answered a bare list (`list_gmail_messages`, `list_payment_reminder_candidates`, `suggest_customers_from_gmail`) now answers `{"items": [...], "provenienza": ...}`, as the paged lists already do, and each of the three resources opens with the sentence. | Telemetry carries the shape of a call, never its content. A bare list has nowhere to put a sentence and one per item would cost the context window N times, so the list is wrapped once. A new tool that returns stored or external text is added to `FREE_TEXT_TOOLS`; a name that is not a registered tool fails `test_mcp_provenance.py`. |
| 2026-11-02 | The 2026-09-12 PostHog row measures the hub «behind a login, no banner», but the hub's chooser, wizards and `/team` pages are anonymous, and `privacy.html` promises «Se dici no, non succede». Does a visitor who said no on the website get recorded the moment they follow a button into the hub? (HWEB-A-01, REB-890) | No. `initAnalytics({ requireConsent })` keeps the hub silent on its public routes until `rebase.consent` (the website's key, same origin) is `granted`; a person who signs in is measured regardless, which is the 2026-09-12 legitimate-interest case, and a logout stops it again unless they had consented. The CRM sets no `requireConsent`. Public-wizard analytics now count consented visitors only, and `privacy.html` says so. | Lorenzo, 2026-11-02
| 2026-11-02 | The hub's web client was 2357 lines of hand-written types and unchecked `request<T>()` casts, so a backend field change never failed its build, while the CRM already generates its client. Keep it by hand? (HWEB-A-03, REB-893) | No: `src/lib/api-types.ts` is generated by `pnpm --filter hub generate:api` from `app.openapi()` (no server, no database) and `lib/api.ts` calls through `openapi-fetch`, keeping `ApiError`, `requestBlob` and the Italian fallbacks; a CI job regenerates the file and fails on a diff, as `pigrocrm-api-types` does. Where the backend declares a looser type than the pages rely on, a `Narrow<>` overlay in `api.ts` holds the page's type until the response model says it. | Lorenzo, 2026-11-02
| 2026-11-02 | The 2026-09-21 row made new routes English and left the existing Italian names to a separate call. The review of 2026-11-02 counted 57,937 Italian identifier occurrences across the code, the databases, the REST and MCP surfaces, the CLI and the environment. Rename the existing ones too, and how? (REB-897) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-02: everything, as one breaking release with no aliases: tables, columns, enum values, JSON keys, REST paths and fields, query parameters, MCP tool and parameter names, CLI flags, environment variables, modules, files, tests and comments. Old browser routes and the hub's mailed API paths keep answering through redirect stubs and second routes; CRM 0068 and hub 0049 rename the schemas. | Every identifier is English (`docs/glossary.md` is the mapping, `tooling/english-lint` fails a change that adds an Italian one). What a person reads stays Italian: UI copy, messages, OpenAPI and MCP descriptions, mails, PDFs and CSVs, template content and the variables users type, users' custom-field keys, FatturaPA names, download filenames, storage prefixes. A persisted code that reaches a sentence goes through a label map, never printed raw. Supersedes the 2026-09-21 row for the names that existed then. |
| 2026-11-03 | The 2026-11-02 row kept the OpenAPI and MCP descriptions Italian, as text an assistant reads out to an Italian freelancer. Should the prose the code publishes stay Italian? (REB-908) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-03: «anche le descrizioni di mcp e openapi devono essere in inglese. le uniche cose in italiano devono essere nomi propri o sigle italiane intraducibili». The OpenAPI document, the MCP instructions, tool, prompt, resource and parameter descriptions, the prompt bodies, the hints only the MCP layer writes and the CLI's help and output are English; an agent answers the user in the user's language whatever language its tools are described in. | What the code publishes to a developer or an agent is English, with Italian only for proper nouns, Italian terms with no English equivalent (FatturaPA, SdI, INPS, PEC) and UI labels quoted as the user sees them. What a person reads inside the product stays Italian (UI copy, the messages the web apps show, emails, documents, template content and variables, custom-field keys, FatturaPA names). `tooling/english-lint` reads published docstrings and the `description=`, `summary=`, `title=`, `help=` and `instructions=` strings of FastAPI, Pydantic, MCP and argparse calls. Supersedes the 2026-11-02 row on the OpenAPI and MCP descriptions only. | Lorenzo, 2026-11-03
| 2026-11-03 | A deal has one `expected_value`, summed by every commercial figure as one kind of number, so a monthly fee and a project price add up as the same thing. Four questions in one row: how is a deal's value shaped, what is counted for a fee with no end, is it gross or rebase's margin, and does a won deal carry it to a contract? (REB-927) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-03: a deal's value is any combination of one-off amounts, recurring fees (monthly or annual, with or without an end) and day or hour rates; «per quelli senza fine, sì, mettiamo i 12 mesi»; the value is gross; a won deal converts into a linked contract «con una conversione perfetta di tutti gli importi». Design: `projects/pigrocrm/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-03-deal-value-lines-design.md`. | A deal's value is its list of `deal_value_lines`; `expected_value` is derived from them by the deal service and never written by a caller. An open-ended fee counts 12 months; one-off and monthly recurring figures are derived the same way, by one function shared with contracts. Amounts are what the customer pays, VAT excluded. Fees overlap freely on a deal and on a contract; a contract keeps at most one day or hour rate card in force on a date, and so does a deal for its day and hour lines (Lorenzo, 2026-11-03: «per ora lasciamo un solo tipo per contratto»). Converting a won deal writes one rate card per line and the contract gives back the deal's total, one-off and monthly figures exactly; the deal's lines are then locked. |
| 2026-11-04 | A CRM person has one email, one phone and one LinkedIn, a customer one email and one phone, and a hub user one phone beside the login address, while real contacts have several (a personal and a work address, an office line that belongs to the company). Where do several contact points go, how are they told apart, and which one does the CRM write to? (REB-939) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-04: on all four surfaces (CRM people, CRM customers, hub freelancers, hub companies); each point carries a label from a fixed list (lavoro, personale, amministrazione, altro) plus a free note; outgoing CRM mail goes to the primary address. The hub's login address stays the identity and an extra address is never a login or a campaign recipient. Design: `projects/pigrocrm/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-04-contact-points-design.md`. |
| 2026-11-09 | Every PigroCRM space sees the same product, while a solo professional and a company work in completely different ways («sono due esperienze completamente diverse»). How does one product fit both, and what is a space against a legal entity? (REB-952) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-09: two profiles for now, solo (flat-rate or ordinary regime) and company (srl); a short onboarding asks which; the 2026-10-03 design of several legal entities in one space stays. Design: `projects/pigrocrm/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-09-space-profiles-modules-and-the-money-picture-design.md`. | A space is one business and a legal entity is who invoices: the profile belongs to the space, the regime to the legal entity. A space profile sets default modules; a module that is off is gone from the web app, REST, MCP and the jobs, and keeps its data. |
| 2026-11-09 | PigroCRM is to give a real-time picture of money in and out, IVA, taxes and staff costs. Does it keep the books, with double entry? (REB-952) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-09: «il commercialista resta e pigro serve a noi per avere un'idea in tempo reale di tutto»; double entry only if precision truly needs it. | PigroCRM keeps no ledger: the accountant does. Cash comes from documents matched to bank movements, IVA from the registers, profit tax is an estimate that the accountant's real figure replaces. What a model reads from free text is a proposal a person accepts; a structured, checkable document (a FatturaPA XML matching a legal entity, a standard bank statement) is certain data. A fiscal rule a design relies on cites its source or is marked to verify. |
| 2026-11-09 | Matches, contracts and signature are moving from the hub into PigroCRM, and everything designed for both is meant to be driven by agents too. What happens to the MCP surface? (REB-952) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-09: everything is manageable over MCP with the permissions of the user behind the token, and the MCP tools move when a feature moves from one product to the other. | Every write a person can make has an MCP tool and a REST route under the same role checks; what cannot be undone stays behind `mcp_full_access` and the token's `full_access`. A feature that moves between products takes its MCP tools with it and the old ones are removed in the same release, with no alias. |
| 2026-11-09 | PigroCRM on a phone was the desktop layout squeezed. Where does the quick add live, what does the Home lead with, and how is an activity marked done? (REB-956) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-09, on the artifact `pigrocrm-phone-shell`: A and A, the recommended options. A bottom bar of five places, «Home», «Attività», «Deal» («Giornate» for a solo space), a square Watermelon «+» in the centre, «Altro»; the Home leads with the figures and puts today's activities under them; an activity is done by tapping its square, with «Annulla» on the toast. The onboarding and the solo Home stay as drawn. Brief on REB-956; design § B6 of `projects/pigrocrm/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-09-space-profiles-modules-and-the-money-picture-design.md`. | Below the desktop breakpoint PigroCRM navigates from a five-place bottom bar whose centre is the «+», never a floating button over the content. The phone Home shows its figures before its lists. Completing an activity is a visible tap target the width of the row, the same gesture as on desktop, never a swipe alone; every completion can be undone from the toast. |
| 2026-11-09 | Free text in incoming documents and mail needs a reader, and Mastro's server-side extraction and IMAP polling were put out on 2026-10-26 (REB-754). Which readers and which mail roads does the incoming documents inbox have? (REB-960) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-09: «entrambe le cose». Two readers produce the same proposal: PigroCRM's own model, off by default, switched on per space by an admin and configured like the hub's «Modelli AI», and the user's own agent over MCP. IMAP mailboxes are in, space-owned ones IMAP only. Mail bodies may be read for task proposals only when the mailbox's owner switches it on, and only the owner sees those proposals. | Supersedes in part the 2026-10-26 row on REB-754: server-side extraction and IMAP polling are in. The privacy page names the model before an admin can switch it on; bytes still never travel over MCP; the header-only precedent of REB-223 (2026-09-23) is broken for task proposals from an owner's own mailbox and nowhere else. Design: `projects/pigrocrm/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-09-incoming-documents-inbox-design.md`. |
| 2026-11-09 | PigroCRM knows who pays it and nothing about whom it pays: a cost is one row with a free-text supplier, no due date and no payment state, and the same company can be both a customer and a supplier. How are suppliers, their invoices and the IVA cycle modelled? (REB-961) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-09: «si vanno bene procediamo». One registry with roles: the `customers` table becomes `organizations`, one record per company with the roles `customer`, `supplier` and `freelancer`, no duplicates, renamed in one release with no alias. A supplier's invoice is a purchase with a due date, a payment state and withholding, split payment and the TD16 to TD19 integration documents; the full IVA cycle and foreign invoices both ways are in scope, and the accountant keeps the books while PigroCRM gives the real-time picture. | Every existing read of customers filters the `customer` role unless the design names an exception; the purchase writes its due dates to the `due_items` table of REB-962; the Italian fiscal rules it adds are rows in the frame's A5 register. Design: `projects/pigrocrm/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-09-suppliers-purchases-and-iva-design.md` (REB-961). |
| 2026-11-09 | Only an issued invoice has a due date and a paid flag, there is no bank account, statement or movement, and the economic tab's projected cash leaves costs out. How does PigroCRM show when money comes in and goes out, week by week? (REB-962) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-09: «si vanno bene procediamo». Due items, their settlements and the rules that date them are core, always on, because an invoice needs them whatever the module state; the `cash` module owns bank accounts, statements, movements, matching, «Cassa» and the projected balance. Statements arrive from email or by upload first, with an alert when one that should be there is missing, and banking APIs come later; Banco BPM, Revolut Business and Qonto are covered first; what a freelancer is paid, and when, follows each freelancer's contract (REB-963 sets the rule). | Every new table follows the `legal_entity_scope` row-security pattern; with `cash` off, payments are still recorded by hand and the overdue reminders still run. Design: `projects/pigrocrm/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-09-due-items-bank-and-cash-schedule-design.md` (REB-962). |
| 2026-11-09 | rebase's relationship with the freelancers it places (match, letters, signature, hours report, availability, payment) lives in the hub, while its commercial relationship lives in the CRM, and nothing tells rebase when to pay a freelancer or whether one is already busy. Where does the relationship with a freelancer live? (REB-963) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-09: «si vanno bene procediamo». In the CRM, behind the `collaborators` module: a freelancer is an organisation with the role `freelancer`, identified by the hub freelancer id, and the match, the framework agreement and letter, their signature, the hours report, availability and payments move there. Talent search, the talent cloud, team requests, campaigns and referrals stay in the hub admin. When a freelancer is paid depends on their contract: a fixed term, or when the client pays. Every feature is over MCP with the user's permissions, and the hub's MCP tools are removed in the same release with no alias. | Supersedes in part the 2026-10-30 row on REB-865, which kept freelancers, matches and contracts on the hub: those move to the CRM, and the rest of that row (the company's registration and its link to the deal) stands. Design: `projects/pigrocrm/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-09-collaborators-in-the-crm-design.md` (REB-963). |
| 2026-11-09 | PigroCRM estimates tax but has no calendar of what falls due and when, and the law, the taxpayer's parameters and the fiscal year end are mixed together. How does it plan every tax payment before it falls due? (REB-964) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-09: «si vanno bene procediamo». The tax calendar is pack data keyed by tax year: law lives in the pack, the taxpayer's parameters in the fiscal profile and the fiscal year end on the legal entity, and each reading freezes the pack version and the parameters it used. `tax_payment_due` now exists, with the dates the pack carries. | Closes the 2026-10-26 note that left `tax_payment_due` unbuilt. No reversal of ORB-188: `update_fiscal_profile` stays on the agent allowlist. Design: `projects/pigrocrm/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-09-taxes-and-contributions-design.md` (REB-964). |
| 2026-11-09 | PigroCRM has no employee, director, payroll, INAIL, TFR, TFM or expense note, and the only labour cost is a user's hourly cost. How does it plan what staff and directors cost and when? (REB-965) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-09: «si vanno bene procediamo». PigroCRM estimates and plans; the payroll consultant computes, and uploaded payslips and paid F24s replace the estimate. Staff members (employees and directors) belong to a legal entity with the pay terms typed per person; everything that moves cash appears in the cash schedule and accruals appear on the P&L; employees' and directors' expenses go through expense notes. | The payroll coefficients, employer INPS, INAIL and TFR are rows in the tax pack of REB-964; staff are allowed on `it-standard`, `it-company` and `it-flat-rate` (employees only) and never on `non-it`. Design: `projects/pigrocrm/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-09-staff-payroll-and-expenses-design.md` (REB-965). |
| 2026-11-09 | Once supplier invoices, IVA, bank movements, taxes and payroll live in PigroCRM, the accountant needs them for a period in one place, and today only the issued-invoice year register CSV exists. What does PigroCRM hand the accountant, and how? (REB-966) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-09: «si vanno bene procediamo». One period export per legal entity (a month, a quarter or a year): a single ZIP with an index, the IVA registers, settlements, bank movements, open items, taxes paid and the original files, each part present only when its module and pack apply, after a readiness checklist whose snapshot goes in the index. It is made and delivered by an admin only, as a download, a share link with an expiry, a download count and revocation, or a file in the space's Drive folder; payroll parts never travel on a plain link. | There is no accountant role and no invitation path: a `readonly` member sees more than an export. The year register CSV stays as it is, and exports are kept for 13 months. Design: `projects/pigrocrm/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-09-accountant-export-design.md` (REB-966). |
| 2026-11-09 | The incoming documents inbox adds two surfaces, «In arrivo» and «Caselle email». How does the list work on a desktop, how does a ZIP of many files show, what happens to the «Gmail» settings tab, who is told when a mailbox needs a person, what happens after an upload, and who sees a personal mailbox? (REB-973) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-09, on the artifact `pigrocrm-inbox-and-mailboxes`: A on all six, the recommended options. A table with the open item in a panel beside it; a ZIP is flat rows, one per file, each with a «da archivio.zip» chip and a filter, the ZIP itself the parent item under «Archiviati»; the «Gmail» tab becomes «Caselle email» in «Collegamenti» with Gmail as one row; a persistent band on every page, raised only when a person must act (`auth_failed`, and the Gmail banner reasons that exist today); after an upload the rows appear at once and fill in, and a refused file stays a red row with its reason; only the owner sees a personal mailbox. Brief: `docs/design/2026-11-09-inbox-and-mailboxes-brief.md`. | «In arrivo» is a table with a side panel on desktop and one column of cards on the phone, and a ZIP never becomes a grouped or nested row. «Caselle email» replaces the Gmail tab, and a personal mailbox is listed to its owner alone, admins included. The shell band extends `GmailBanner` to every mailbox that needs a person (`auth_failed`, and the Gmail reasons `revoked`, `expired`, `expiring`, `scope_missing`), has no close control, and is never raised for `failing` or `disconnected`. A ZIP, like a PEC envelope, stays a parent item under «Archiviati», and the origin chip and filter are how its files are found. A refused upload is a red row with a reason, never only a toast. |
| 2026-11-09 | The two new surfaces need a loading state, while Lorenzo's general taste file says skeletons shaped like the content, the application variant says a skeleton shaped like the row, and the repo has one shared loader. Which does «In arrivo» and «Caselle email» use? (REB-973) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-09: the custom loader the app already has, not a skeleton or a new spinner. That is `Loader` of `@rebase/ui/loader`, the brand's four-tile mark of the 2026-09-25 row. | The loading state of «In arrivo» and «Caselle email» is `Loader` with a `role="status"` sentence beside it («Leggo i nuovi arrivi…» and «Leggo le caselle…»), `size-4` inline or a larger `className` for a whole-panel wait, as `ProposalDetail` does. No `Skeleton`, no spinner of another shape, on either surface; a file being read in an uploaded row shows the same `Loader`. |
| 2026-11-09 | A due item's settlement needs a different permission by direction and by source (a client's payment is a collaborator's, money going out and every tax, IVA, withholding or payroll item an administrator's), but an MCP tool is registered for everyone or for nobody, and `FULL_ACCESS_ACTIONS` was equal to the tools hidden without the flag. How are the due item tools gated? (REB-967) | The four tools (`list_due_items`, `get_due_item`, `settle_due_item`, `reverse_settlement`) are on every token's surface and `DueItemService` classifies the call from the record: an incoming ordinary item is `settle_due_item` (collaborator, agent-allowed), an outgoing or fiscal one is `settle_outgoing_due_item` (administrator, `full_access` for an agent), the same pair for undoing, and the fiscal kinds are read under `read_fiscal_due_items`. A list leaves the fiscal kinds out for a caller who may not read them; naming one is refused. Credits are negative items with negative payments, and a dependent due item names a dependency of its own legal entity. | The set of `FULL_ACCESS_ACTIONS` is the banned tools, the REST-only operations and, since this row, `FULL_ACCESS_BY_THE_RECORD` in `test_mcp_invoice_ban.py`, with a test that each of its tools is on the default surface. A later source kind that needs the fiscal gate joins `RESTRICTED_SOURCE_KINDS`, and `test_due_items.py` lists every kind. Design: `projects/pigrocrm/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-09-due-items-bank-and-cash-schedule-design.md` § 1 and § 5. |
| 2026-11-09 | The collaborators design lets a letter pay the freelancer after the client has paid, but no contract text said so, and the ordinary framework agreement's 7.2 says the freelancer's payment does not depend on the client's. Who writes the clause, and what happens to agreements already signed? (REB-979) | Lorenzo, 2026-11-09: write the clause without waiting for a lawyer, and have the contracts reviewed as a whole later. The letter gains the optional field `collection_days` (`giorni-incasso`, 1 to 60). When set, the term the letter prints is «N giorni dopo che il Cliente ha saldato rebase per lo stesso lavoro, e in ogni caso entro 60 giorni dal ricevimento della fattura» («ricevuta» on the occasional letter), followed by a paragraph, «Pagamento a incasso», that says the client's payment decides when rebase pays and never whether, that rebase pays by the 60 days whatever the client does, and that the letter itself is the parties' consent for this engagement, an exception to 7.2 when the signed framework agreement does not yet allow it. Both framework agreements go to version 1.1, and their 7.2 says a letter may tie the term to the client's payment within the 60 days with the risk staying rebase's. | A letter may wait for the client's payment only within the 60 days of law 81/2017 and with the risk staying rebase's, which is what keeps 7.2's substance. An agreement signed at 1.0 is neither re-sent nor invalidated: it keeps the text it was signed with (article 17.3) and the admin page only shows «Nuova versione disponibile»; the letter carries the consent, so no framework needs re-signing for it. The wording is written on Lorenzo's go-ahead and awaits the overall legal review of the contracts: any change to it is a new text version. The letter's paragraph is conditional on the field (`{{se giorni-incasso}}` and `{{fine se}}` in the Markdown), so a letter without the field prints today's term and no clause. |

| 2026-11-09 | The ceiling sums `taxable_amount` of invoices paid in a year, but a settlement is gross and can be partial, and the due item replaces `payment_date` as the date of a payment. (REB-968) | Spec 2026-11-09 (due items), § 1.1: each settlement counts by its own date, for its share of the invoice's `taxable_amount` (`taxable_amount * settlement / item amount`, summed before rounding), so a payment in full is exactly the taxable amount and instalments across 31 December are split between the years. An invoice paid with no `payment_date` is settled on its issue date, the fallback every reader already had, and an invoice with a zero total has no item and nothing to collect. | `fiscal/ceiling.py` and `monthly_paid` under `collection` read `due_item_settlements`; `reconcile_sales_invoices` in `ensure-space-defaults` writes the missing items and only those. |

(REB-984) | Three acts, each on an item that waits (`new` or `proposed`): discard with a reason, mark as the copy of another item the caller sees, and say what it is and for which legal entity. A proposal waiting on the item is a person's to decide: a person's act rejects it, an agent's is refused. | 2026-11-10 | An srl runs on `it-standard`, so it has no IRES or IRAP. How does the company get its own pack and when do existing srl entities move to it? (REB-1005) | `it-company` is a fiscal pack beside `it-standard`: RF01, the same regime strategy (`pack_ids` on the strategy lists both packs), no ceiling, no charge, and a `CompanyTaxLaw` holding the two verified rates (IRES 24%, art. 77 TUIR; IRAP ordinary 3.90%, art. 16 D.Lgs. 446/1997 as the Finanze page and the regions' 2026 tables state it, region variance up to 0.92 points), entered from 2026. Existing active entities on `it-standard` whose denomination says they are an srl (art. 2463 n. 2 c.c.) move on 1 January and never mid-year, by `FiscalYearRoll` inside the daily `pigrocrm automations` pass, which already walks every space and is watched for lateness; it saves the profile through `FiscalProfileService.upsert`, so the estimates of the pack being left are kept. The space profile is not used: a space can hold a person on the ordinary regime beside an srl. The onboarding writes `it-company` for an srl. The year register groups by regime code, so a year that spans the move is one RF01 row, named by the pack of its latest invoice. | A pack that invoices like another lists itself in the regime strategy's `pack_ids` and never gets a second strategy. A pack move that must not happen mid-year is a step of the daily pass that acts on 1 January only, not a migration and not a new cron line; one refused entity is counted on `stderr` and does not stop the others. Normattiva's printed IRAP rate is not the rate (A5 row), and the IRAP rate a company pays depends on its region and activity (A5 row «IRAP region rules», to verify), so no figure is computed from it until that row is verified. |

Typing the same tax and year again replaces the three together, and nothing is ever computed from or into a declared figure. `get_fiscal_estimate` answers the figures beside the estimate with a `reading` of `estimate` or `declared`; a newer estimate is written by another path and leaves them alone. | An administrator, or a token with `full_access`, types a figure through REST `PUT /api/analytics/fiscal/declared` or the privileged tool `set_declared_tax_figure`; the amounts stay out of the timeline. Reading a figure from a document, recomputing advances and the due items are their own cards. |
| 2026-11-10 | The due-items design says a forecast of money in is replaced by its document, but nothing on an invoice line says which contract period it bills, a draft has no forecast kind of its own, and the design leaves open how far ahead a fee is materialised and what a contract billed on completion with no end is dated. How are they settled? (REB-997) | An invoice line may carry the contract rate card it bills and the day its period starts, both or neither (`InvoiceLineIn`, `invoice_lines.rate_card_id` and `period_start`): `bind_work_units` sets them and a hand-made fee invoice names them, the card having to belong to a live contract of the document's customer. A line covers the occurrence of that card whose span, up to the next occurrence, holds its `period_start`. A draft, a draft proforma and a confirmed proforma are forecast under their own source kind `sales_draft`, so the readers that join `sales_invoice` to its invoice never meet one. Fee occurrences are materialised 180 days ahead and a forecast dated in the past is dropped. The worked days of a contract billed on completion with no `end` are forecast undated, never on an invented date. The daily job is a step of `pigrocrm automations`, not a fifth cron. | A forecast is keyed by a uuid5 of source kind, rate card or contract and period, with a partial unique index; every writer takes the contract row first, so a job and an issue queue and end with one item. The forecast amount is the invoice's gross total, and withholding and split payment arrive with the real invoice. A contract that is not `active`, a renewal and a deal are never forecast (`projects/pigrocrm/AGENTS.md`). |

| 2026-11-10 | The suppliers design says closing an IVA period blocks every write that changes a register, but not which date places a document, whether the period must have ended or been settled, how a refusal reads, which acts are left alone, or how the page keeps `?period=` honest when the sidebar's legal entity changes its frequency. What does REB-1036 build? (REB-1036) | `iva_period_locks` (migration 0101) holds one row per legal entity, frequency, year and number with the days it covers; `IvaPeriodLockService` closes (an unscoped administrator, or a `full_access` token) and reopens, each with a timeline entry on one id per period (`iva_period_closed`, `iva_period_reopened`), and reopening deletes the row. A period must have ended to be closed, as to be settled, and closing settles it in the same transaction, so what is frozen is what the registers add up to now; a settlement a payment froze that would change refuses the close with the reason. A purchase invoice or credit note is placed by its `iva_period` (create, update from and to, cancel, delete, restore), a sale by its issue date (issue, annul, import); a receipt, an expense, a draft and a proforma are in no register, and a payment, the transmission mark and the settlement itself change none, so none is refused. The refusal is a 409 naming the period, over REST and MCP alike. The page honours `?period=` only with its `?frequency=`, and drops a period of another frequency from the URL; a period that has not ended offers no button. The monthly `period_locks` stays the hours' and the cost side of purchases', and neither implies the other. |
| 2026-11-10 | The cash design lists `match_movement` as a collaborator's act an agent may run, the card says agents only propose, and the brief draws every movement screen for an administrator. (REB-1042) | A proposal is written by the importer (`system`, only for the one fit with evidence besides the amount, never settling) or by an agent with `full_access` (`propose_movement_match`); confirming and undoing are a person's and an administrator's. Confirming settles each item dated on the movement's booking date with the movement as the payment's evidence; a payment already marked by hand for the same amount is adopted, not doubled; a match covers the movement exactly in one currency, a last item may be paid in part only when a person says so. | An agent never settles from a bank movement (`confirm_movement_match` and `undo_movement_match` are person-only). | 2026-11-11 | The taxes design left «whether INPS contributions are deducted before the substitute tax» of the flat-rate estimate to verify, and the code took the contributions on the income less the substitute tax. In what order does the law apply them, and what does the estimate do with a profitability coefficient that is not in the law's table? (REB-1066) | L. 190/2014 art. 1 c. 64 and the Agenzia's quadro LM instructions settle it: the contributions are owed on the flat-rate income, are deducted from it (`si deducono dal reddito determinato ai sensi del presente comma`), and the substitute tax rate is applied to what is left. The estimate now computes `contributions = income x INPS rate` and `substitute tax = (income - contributions) x rate`; the total to set aside and the net income are the same as before to within two cents (each formula rounds twice), the split between the two lines moves (on 100,000 of revenue at 67%, 5% and 26.07%: substitute tax 3,350.00 to 2,476.66, INPS 16,593.56 to 17,466.90). Without an INPS rate the substitute tax has no figure, since a figure computed as if nothing were deducted is a guess. The estimate also echoes `substitute_tax_base`. A coefficient that is not one of annex 4's (40, 54, 62, 67, 78, 86) is a note in the estimate (`coefficient_warning`) and never a refusal, so no saved profile stops working. Each regime's income basis (person cash, srl accrual, flat rate cash), annex 4 by ATECO 2007 group, the 5% and 15% rates with the start-up conditions and the substitute tax advances are pack data in `fiscal/law_flat_rate.py` and `fiscal/law_income_basis.py`. | A fiscal rule is read from the primary source and carries its article and wording in the pack before an estimate relies on it; a rule that changes a figure an estimate has shown says so in the pull request with a worked example. A profile value outside the law's table is warned about, not refused, unless a later card decides otherwise. |
| 2026-11-11 | `/api/analytics/fiscal` and the MCP `get_fiscal_estimate` summed the invoices issued in the year, the economic overview passed the settlements of the year, and the pack states a different basis per regime (cash for a person and the flat-rate regime, accrual for an srl). Which revenue and costs does an estimate read, and what does a figure say it was computed from? (REB-1071) | One function, `fiscal/basis.py::year_basis`, reads the basis the pack's law names for the year and every path reads it. **Cash**: the taxable share of every settlement of the year by its own date (the ceiling's reading), less the collected share of the INPS surcharge lines; for the ordinary person, costs are the purchases paid in the year and the payroll items settled in it. **Accrual**: invoices by competence (`coalesce(accrual_from, issue_date)`) in the financial year closing in the year by the legal entity's own year end, purchases by document date, staff and director cost as the staff module accrues it, told apart for IRAP (REB-1073). A year the pack states no basis for has no revenue and no figure. The payload carries `basis`: the period, the revenue, the costs by kind (none for the flat-rate regime, whose income is revenue times a coefficient), what was left out and why (`not_collected`, `inps_surcharge`, `shared_purchases`, `staff_cost_not_estimated`) and what was counted on a stand-in (a purchase with no due item counts at its document date, since no purchase writes due items yet). The economic overview's `tax` is `get_fiscal_estimate` itself; its `projected_tax` stays a «what if» on projected revenue and names no basis. **A flat-rate figure moves**: an invoice of 1000.00 issued on 10 December 2026 and paid on 15 January 2027 was 2026's revenue and is now 2027's, so 2026's taxable amount at 67% falls by 670.00 and 2027's rises by the same; an issued invoice nobody has paid is no revenue yet, and the estimate lists it as `not_collected`. The INPS surcharge question stays on REB-975. Sales credit notes are not netted: the register holds none (PigroCRM does not issue them and imports none). | The two bases are the law's (art. 54 and 66 TUIR, art. 83 and 109 TUIR, L. 190/2014 art. 1 c. 64, read 2026-11-11, `fiscal/law_income_basis.py`), so choosing one in a service would be the defect this removes. The estimate is only for a year in which the law has an entry: `it-standard` and `it-company` start in 2026 and a year before it says nothing, as the pack does. A cash year is the calendar year (a person's tax year); an srl's follows its own year end. |

A difference left by an advance already paid is carried to the next item of that tax (the second advance, then the balance, which is the tax less what was actually paid) and its description says «da confermare»: the legal treatment is to verify, and there is no confirm action yet. | A tax due item is written only by `TaxDueItemService`; its source kinds `tax_balance` and `tax_advance` are restricted (an administrator whose scope covers the legal entity, or a `full_access` agent; the schedule and the Home leave them out for anybody else); a figure or parameter that is missing gives no item and the report says why (`TaxItemsReport.not_written`). |

Person: 40/60, 50/50 under ISA, no advance at or below €51.65, one November payment below €257.52 (up to €206 under ISA). Flat-rate: 50/50, none at or below €51.65, one payment up to €206. srl: the same 40/60 and 50/50, none at or below €20.66, IRES in one payment up to €103; no single-payment limit is stated for IRAP by a source read, so its advance is always split (to verify, REB-975). A person's and an srl's split needs the profile's ISA flag, and a profile that does not state it has no advance. A company's balance and first advance fall on the last day of the sixth month after its fiscal year end and its second advance on the last day of the eleventh month of the period (`CompanyAdvanceLaw`), for a year end that is the last day of a month; a tax year is named by the calendar year its period starts in. | `fiscal/advances.py` holds the lookups and the pack holds every figure with its source; INPS contributions, credits (a negative balance is no item), instalment plans and F24 groups are their own cards and are not written here. |

The pack has no law for that earlier year, so the fee year's accrual rule reads it. The date is the first income-tax advance's (30 June for a calendar year), the F24 code 3850 is in the description. No province, an unmapped one (BT, SU) or a year with no fee law gives no item and a `TaxItemNotWritten` reason. | 2026-11-11 | How is an srl's IRES and IRAP estimated from what PigroCRM holds (invoices, purchases, staff records), what does a figure say it assumes, and what happens when the address or the contract type is not known? (REB-1073) | `fiscal/company_estimate.py` reads the year's accrual `year_basis` and the pack's `CompanyTaxLaw`. **IRES** is 24% of revenue less purchases, staff cost and director fees, with no variation of the return (non-deductible costs, the IRAP deduction on staff cost, depreciation); a loss gives zero and says so, and no loss is carried forward. **IRAP** is the value of production (revenue less purchases, staff and director cost stay in the base), less the art. 11 c. 4-bis small-base deduction the pack carries, times the rate of the region of the legal entity's address (`place.region_code`; `BZ` or `TN` by province); the deduction for permanent employees (c. 4-octies) and the per-employee one (c. 4-bis.1) are **not applied** because a staff member carries no permanent-contract flag, and the payload says the base is overstated by that cost. An address that names no region gives no IRAP figure and `missing` names the address; the region is never guessed. Both are lines of the readings (`ires`, `irap`), a declared figure outranks them, the estimate sits in `FiscalEstimate.company` with `lines`, `base`, `rate`, `amount` and `assumptions` per tax, `due_total` is the sum once both are known, and a frozen year keeps `company` and the region (`FiscalEstimateReading.parameters.irap_region`). `TaxYearLaw.has_income_estimate` is now true for a pack with `company_tax.irap`. The economic overview draws IRES, IRAP and the total for an srl and no projection or net (those need costs a «what if» revenue does not have). |
| 2026-11-11 | When does an admin hear that a tax falls due, and when that a declared figure is missing? (REB-1078) | `tax_payment_due`: one reminder per open `tax_balance`, `tax_advance` or `tax_instalment` due item, `warning` 14 days and `serious` 3 days before its date, to the admins whose scope covers the legal entity; a date already past is left to the schedule, which draws it overdue. `tax_figure_missing`: one per legal entity, income tax and tax year, `warning` the day after the pack's return filing date (`return_filing_date`) and `serious` 30 days after, while no declared figure exists; only the latest tax year already filed is looked at and a legal entity created after the filing date is not asked, so no old year is invented; a tax with no filing rule raises nothing. Both sit under the `taxes` module, and titles and details name the tax and the date and carry no amount, which stays in the app. | The ladders are the catalogue's (design § 4) and the 30 days of the second rung is ours, as every default is changeable in the settings. Chamber fee and notice plan items join `TAX_DUE_KINDS` when their source kinds exist. |
| 2026-11-11 | How does an instalment plan split the June tax items, what happens to it once a payment is on it, and how is a notice from the tax office entered? (REB-1075) | A plan is one row per legal entity, tax and tax year of the balance (`tax_instalment_plans`) holding only the number of instalments; the pack gives the dates and the interest (`instalment_schedule`, whose test is the Agenzia's own table), and the last instalment is the 16 December of the year the return is filed (`plan_math.plan_schedule`, from `return_filing_date`). It covers that balance and the first advance of the next tax year, which fall on the same day; the November advance is never split. Each of the two becomes one `tax_instalment` item per instalment (equal principal, the last taking the cent, the schedule's interest on top) in place of its single item, and the REB-1074 refresh writes the instalments and never the single item again. After a payment, even a part of one, on an instalment, none of the payment's instalments is rewritten, so a partly paid one keeps its remainder, and the plan is refused a change or a removal (409) until the payment is reversed; a payment made in one go before the plan is left as it is. The difference a paid advance leaves is not carried over an advance split in instalments. A notice plan is typed by hand (`tax_notice_plans`: label, tax, total before interest, a day and an interest in euro per instalment), writes `notice_plan` items (restricted, module `taxes`, state `expected`), and is refused a change or a deletion after a payment in the same way. REST `PUT /api/analytics/fiscal/instalment-plan`, `GET .../plans`, `POST/PUT/DELETE .../notice-plans`, and the privileged MCP tools `set_tax_instalment_plan`, `create_tax_notice_plan`, `update_tax_notice_plan`, `delete_tax_notice_plan`, `list_tax_plans` (`full_access`). | A rewrite around a paid instalment would move money already paid, and refusing is undone by reversing the payment. The pack's rule for an srl with a broken fiscal year and the flat-rate regime's plan stay «to verify» in A5 (REB-975). | A plan item is never recomputed after a payment touched its payment, and a plan with a payment on it is never changed or removed by a service. |
| 2026-11-11 | The ordinary regime owed no estimate: how are IRPEF, its two surcharges and INPS computed for a person, in what order, from what, and what happens to a fact the law needs and the profile does not state? (REB-1072) | `fiscal/ordinary_estimate.py`, on the cash basis of REB-1071. Income is collected less paid (art. 54 TUIR, a loss gives zero); INPS is computed first on it for the profile's category (`FiscalPack.inps_for`); the contributions are deducted from the total income (art. 10 c. 1 lett. e TUIR, read on Normattiva and cited in `IrpefLaw.social_security_deduction`; the year's own computed contributions stand in for those paid in the period, as the flat-rate estimate does); IRPEF by brackets on the rest; the regional surcharge of the region of the legal entity's address (`BZ`/`TN` by province) and the municipal one of its cadastral code, the latter with the exemption threshold first and the brackets by slice (the threshold is verified in art. 1 c. 11 D.L. 138/2011, the by-slice reading is the Agenzia's «con gli stessi criteri previsti per l'Irpef», to verify); both only when the IRPEF is due, which with no detraction modelled is the gross IRPEF, said in the payload (art. 13 TUIR is not applied: an upper bound); the withholdings the customers kept on the settlements of the year, by the year `WithholdingCreditLaw` says, are credited against the gross IRPEF alone, and an excess is a `credit`, never a negative tax. The readings' `irpef` is that net IRPEF (the advance base: the regional surcharge has no advance and the municipal one its own 30%), `inps` the contributions; the surcharges are `parts` of the `person` payload, and `with_surcharges` and `due_total` carry the totals that include them, `None` until both surcharges are known, so a missing municipal rate no longer takes the IRPEF itself away. A missing fact gives no figure and is named: no INPS category (or `inps_covered_elsewhere`, `inps_trade`, and `inps_before_1996` only where the two ceilings differ) means no INPS and no IRPEF (a base with no contributions deducted would overstate it), no address means no surcharge, and a municipal row that is `pending` (the 2026 list of 11 November has no deliberation for Milano, Roma and 3,793 others: the Finanze shows the same `0*` until 20 December) gives no municipal figure and no total with surcharges; a regional exemption tested on the «reddito complessivo» (Valle d'Aosta) that the base alone would wrongly grant gives no regional figure. The previous year's rate is not borrowed in code, because the Finanze's own rule carries it over only after 20 December and on its own list, which the next import of the list brings. A year frozen when the entity left another pack is now served whenever the frozen pack is not the current one (the ordinary pack's law covers 2026 too, and a flat-rate year must not be recomputed as an IRPEF year). The estimate of a what-if `revenue` carries no `person`, and the economic overview draws no projection or net for it. |
| 2026-11-11 | How are tax credits recorded, how does an F24 group settle with one movement, and what happens to a tax forecast that is grouped? (REB-1076) | A credit is a negative `tax_credit` due item with a `tax_credits` row for its origin (withholding suffered on sales, per the pack's credit-year law, persons only; IVA credit carried forward, only the latest period's). A group needs tax-office items of one date with a positive net; each line freezes the pack's code or reads «codice da verificare» (no IVA, withholding or payroll code is in the pack, none invented). Grouping moves a tax forecast to `expected` (the F24 is the payment, as paying already did) rather than letting the group match regardless of state, so the schedule no longer shows it as a forecast and the matching needs no special case; ungrouping triggers the recompute, which rewrites the forecast. A grouped item is frozen. The bank matching offers an open group whose net equals the movement and settles all its lines, credits at their negative amount; a line is never matched alone. A group that nets to zero or a credit is refused (no movement to match). Receipts are inbox `tax_document` items linked to the group. The withholding credit is not written once a declared IRPEF figure exists, since a declared balance is already net; whether the F24 shows it at all is in A5 to verify. |
| 2026-11-12 | What happens to an F24 group that nets to zero or to a credit, and what happens to a credit that is not used up? (REB-1093) | Such a group is allowed (the 2026-11-11 rule that refused it is lifted): it has no bank movement to match, so a person marks it filed with the day and, optionally, the receipt (`file_f24`, REST `POST /api/f24-groups/{id}/file`; the permission of confirming a match, no agent credential, so no MCP tool, like `confirm_movement_match`). It settles as a match does, through the same payment writer (`DueItemService._record_payment`) and `F24GroupRepository.settle`, without a movement: every debit is paid in full and the credits are spent oldest tax year first until they cover the debits; a net credit spends only part of the last credit it touches and releases the credits it did not need, which stay open and can go in another F24 as a line of what is left (`f24_group_items.used_amount` records the use, `credit_left` is the server's figure). A group with an amount to pay is refused: only the money leaving the account proves it was paid. Reopening a filed group (`reopen_f24`, the filing's undo) reverses the payments it wrote and is refused while the credit it released sits in another F24. The F24 with a zero balance must still be filed, through the Agenzia's telematic services only (D.L. 66/2014 art. 11 c. 2; circolare 27/E 2014; A5 register); the product records it and never files. |
| 2026-11-11 | How does «Tasse» show the due items, the plans and the advance method, and how is a carried difference confirmed? (REB-1079) | «Scadenze» draws each tax due item on its date with its own amount and state pill; a date's net waits for the F24 groups, since no server figure sums a date and the browser adds none. A day with items drops the pack's line for the taxes the items cover. «Rate» is the third tab, a table (cards on a phone) of the instalment plans and the notice plans with a panel; «Nuovo piano» enters a notice's plan and sits inside the tab, not in the page header, because in «Tutte le aziende» each entity has its own tab content and the plan belongs to one entity. The advance method and the June instalments are changed from the tax's panel in «Imposte» (`FiscalEstimate.advance_method` says the method in force). The difference an advance already paid leaves on the next item (REB-1074's «da confermare») is confirmed by an administrator with `POST /api/due-items/{id}/carry-confirmation`: one nullable timestamp, `due_items.carry_confirmed_at`, kept while the item is unchanged and cleared when the item is rewritten, recorded in the timeline as `tax_carry_confirmed`; no MCP tool. The Home keeps reading the calendar, not due items by kind, so it is unchanged. | The brief puts the strong action in the header; with several entities that cannot say whose plan. The legal treatment of a carried difference stays «to verify»: the screen states no rule. |
| 2026-11-11 | How does «Tasse» show the due items, the plans and the advance method, and how is a carried difference confirmed? (REB-1079) | «Scadenze» draws each tax due item on its date with its own amount and state pill; a date's net waits for the F24 groups, since no server figure sums a date and the browser adds none. A day with items drops the pack's line for the taxes the items cover. «Rate» is the third tab, a table (cards on a phone) of the instalment plans and the notice plans with a panel; «Nuovo piano» enters a notice's plan and sits inside the tab, not in the page header, because in «Tutte le aziende» each entity has its own tab content and the plan belongs to one entity. The advance method and the June instalments are changed from the tax's panel in «Imposte» (`FiscalEstimate.advance_method` says the method in force). The difference an advance already paid leaves on the next item (REB-1074's «da confermare») is confirmed by an administrator with `POST /api/due-items/{id}/carry-confirmation`: a person's decision (no agent credential, `full_access` or not: the legal treatment is «to verify»), one nullable timestamp, `due_items.carry_confirmed_at`, kept while a rewrite leaves the description (so the difference) the same and asking again when the difference changes, recorded in the timeline as `tax_carry_confirmed`; no MCP tool. The Home keeps reading the calendar, not due items by kind, so it is unchanged. | The brief puts the strong action in the header; with several entities that cannot say whose plan. The legal treatment of a carried difference stays «to verify»: the screen states no rule. |
| 2026-11-11 | Tax rules change every year. How does PigroCRM keep each year's law, and what stops a later edit from changing a past year's figures? | The fiscal pack holds each section the way the law changes it: statutory sections (`laws`, `calendars`, `payment_rules`, `ceilings`, `charges`) carry forward from their `from_year`, and yearly figures (`inps`, `irpef`, `chamber_fees`, IRAP rates, municipal lists, legal interest) are read by exact year and give no figure for a year with no entry (REB-1083). A committed ledger, `fiscal/law_ledger.json`, holds a fingerprint of every section of every pack for every year that has law, and a test refuses a change to a recorded year unless a correction entry (pack, section, year, primary source, reason) is in the same diff; a new year is an addition and passes (REB-1084). | A new year's law is a new entry, never an edit of an earlier one. A year that is recorded in the ledger is changed only by a declared correction with its source. Every year is recorded with `python -m pigrocrm.core.fiscal.law_ledger --add-year` in the PR that adds it, following `projects/pigrocrm/docs/yearly-tax-law.md`. A municipal list whose sidecar says it is not final is not recorded yet. |
| 2026-11-12 | Does the Home's tax block show the tax due items, and for whom? (REB-1095) | Yes: it reads the same queries as «Tasse» (`useTaxDueItems`, the open and paid items of the calendar year, the next year too when fewer than two dates are left) and the same merge (`groupByDueDate`: a pack line stays only for a tax with no item that day, a paid item counting as covering it). It then draws the first two days that still have something to pay, an overdue item's day first: each open item with its own amount and state pill, each uncovered pack line with «non calcolabile». The items are gated by `read_fiscal_due_items`, separately from `get_tax_calendar`: a role that reads the calendar and not the items sees the calendar alone and the items are not requested; a failed read of the items leaves the dates drawn, not an error. This replaces the REB-1079 line «the Home keeps reading the calendar». | A date without its amount told the owner a payment was near but not how much, while «Tasse» already knew. The dates stay true when the items cannot be read, so one failed read does not blank the block. |
| 2026-11-12 | The tax estimates disclosed gaps (REB-1100): is the 4% INPS surcharge in the flat-rate revenue, which chamber covers BT and SU, what does a company pay in the year it registers, and does a 2026 decree move the 30 June dates? | The 4% surcharge is collected revenue like the fee: it counts in the coefficient's base and in the 85,000 euro limit, and `StatutoryCharge` loses its second flag, so the revenue the ceiling reads and the coefficient is applied to is one figure (art. 1 c. 212 L. 662/1996, INPS messaggio 7751/2012, the Agenzia's guide to the quadro LM; circolari 10/E 2016 and 9/E 2019 say nothing about it). BT is Bari's and SU and VS Cagliari-Oristano's (the chambers' national portal, D.P.R. 183/2026 renames SU and adds VS, OG, OT; OG and OT stay unmapped). A company that registered in the year pays the first tier's fixed amount (120 euro in a 20% chamber) 30 days after its application, so `legal_entities.registration_date` is a new optional field in the Azienda form. The deferral of art. 1-sexies D.L. 63/2026 moves the 2026 balance and first advance of the income taxes, IRAP and the chamber fee to 20 July for those it covers; the pack states it for 2026 only (the 2027 calendar restores 30 June) and reads every taxpayer as covered. The pack also carries the Agenzia's codes for the periodic IVA, 1001, 1040 and DM10, each F24 line with the month it pays for. | A rule a source states goes in the pack and the register with its citation, and a ledger correction names what moved; a fact the product cannot read from its records (the registration date) is asked once, optional, and an unanswered one reads as an older company. The deferral covers an unknown share of taxpayers (an activity with no approved ISA is outside): it is on the accountant's list rather than guessed. |
| 2026-11-12 | How does an earlier year's tax loss reduce a later year's IRES and a person's IRPEF, where does the loss come from, and what happens when a year in between is unknown? (REB-1088) | The pack's statutory section `loss_carry` (`fiscal/law_loss_carry.py`, from 2026) holds the rules with their sources, read on Normattiva on 2026-11-12: an srl carries a loss without a time limit against 80% of each later year's taxable income (art. 84 c. 1 TUIR) and the losses of the first three periods of a new activity against all of it (c. 2); an ordinary person carries a business loss the same way (art. 8 c. 3, which applies art. 84 c. 2) and a loss of an art or a profession not at all (art. 8 c. 1). Losses come from `tax_losses` (migration 0119, `TaxLossService`, REST `PUT`/`DELETE /api/analytics/fiscal/losses`, the tools `set_tax_loss` and `delete_tax_loss`, the permission of a declared figure): one row per legal entity, tax (`ires` or `irpef`) and year of origin with the amount, the remainder at the end of a later year and the two facts of c. 2 (`new_activity`, `activity_start_year`), because the product holds neither the date of constitution nor whether the activity is new. An srl's earlier year that closes in an estimated loss with no typed loss for it is carried as a fallback and labelled estimated; a person's is not, since the pack's basis is a professional's. The remainder rolls forward year by year on each year's own estimated income (oldest loss first, which the law does not fix); a year in between with no income to read stops the roll and the tax has no figure until a remainder is typed. The 100% needs the start year and the new-activity fact; without them the general 80% stands and the assumptions say so. The INPS base is not reduced, and the c. 3 change-of-control limit and the exempt-income reduction are not modelled; the estimate says both. The new section is an addition to the ledger, not a correction of a recorded year. |
| 2026-11-12 | Is a withholding suffered, or an IVA credit a period leaves, a credit due item and a line of an F24? (REB-1100; reverses the credit rule of the 2026-11-11 REB-1076 row, which stays as written) | No. A withholding suffered is deducted inside the income-tax return (RN33 col. 4 into RN34), so the IRPEF estimate and the advance base are already net of it and a credit item beside them counted it twice in the schedule; the IVA credit of a period is taken in by the next periodic settlement (`credit_in`), and the Agenzia's periodic codes say «importi a credito compensati: NON COMPILARE». `TaxCreditService` is removed: no code writes a `tax_credit` item or a `tax_credits` row, for a person, a flat-rate holder or an srl (whose IRES is net since REB-1098). Migration 0116 drops the open `tax_credit` items of the origins `withholding_suffered` and `iva_carry_forward` (`dropped`, the due items' own state), deletes their rows and dissolves the open F24 groups that held them; a credit that is a line of a settled group or has a payment on it is left and logged by id. The `tax_credits` table, the credit-line machinery of `F24GroupService` (REB-1076, REB-1093) and the restricted kind stay for a credit a return shows and a person uses in compensation (art. 17 D.Lgs. 241/1997), which needs its own origin and a typed entry when it is built | A tax that a return or a settlement already nets is never also a due item of the other sign. An F24 credit is a compensation a person makes of a credit the return shows, never something the product derives from invoices or settlements (A5, REB-1100) |
| 2026-11-12 | An srl with places of business in more than one region: how does the IRAP base get split, and what happens when the data to split it is missing? (REB-1092) | By art. 4 c. 2 D.Lgs. 446/1997 and the instructions to quadro IR: in proportion to the gross pay of the staff assigned to each site, a person counting for the site's region from three calendar months of service in the period, the rest going to the registered office's region. The registered office is the legal entity's own address, not a row; a site is a `legal_entity_sites` row (region, optional city and address) and a staff member works at a site or at the registered office (`staff_members.site_id` / `at_registered_office`, never both). The rule applies only when a site is in another region than the registered office's, so one site, none or sites in its own region compute as before. A person with no workplace stated stops the split: no regional figure, `missing` names them, never a guess. | Writing sites is a person's act (`update_legal_entity_sites`, person-only) and no MCP tool lists them, so the staff tool does not offer the workplace fields. The calendar-month count of «three months» and the single workplace for the whole period are readings, said in the payload; staff seconded to or from third parties and the regions' own deductions and credits are not modelled. | Lorenzo's card REB-1092, the readings the implementing agent's, 2026-11-12 |
